, we see that we indeed have the condition

\frac{\xi+2\eta k+\xi k}{2}=\frac{\xi(7k+1)}{2}<1,

assuming
\xi<\frac{1}{8k}
, implying that every permutation minimizing
\|\widetilde{\Theta}_{j}J-\widetilde{\Theta}_{j^{*}}\|_{0}
agrees with the composition of the optimal alignment of
\widetilde{\Theta}_{j}
to
\Theta
and the inverse optimal alignment of
\widetilde{\Theta}_{j^{*}}
to
\Theta
. Therefore, after a global relabeling, the majority vote over the good estimates may be analyzed as if each
\widetilde{\Theta}_{j}
were optimally aligned directly to
\Theta
.

For each
j
, let
J_{\widetilde{\Theta}_{j}}
denote the optimal alignment of
\widetilde{\Theta}_{j}
to
\Theta
. Consider any fixed community. By assumption, for each
j\in[\ell]
, the matrix
\widetilde{\Theta}_{j}J_{\widetilde{\Theta}_{j}}-\Theta
has at most
\xi n
nonzero entries, of which at most
\xi m
correspond to nodes in that community, where
m=\frac{n}{k}
. Thus, among
\{\widetilde{\Theta}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\ell}
, at most
\ell(\xi m)
nodes are misclassified by
any
of the
\ell
estimates. The remaining nodes in the community would certainly be classified correctly by a majority vote. Since this holds for all communities, we conclude that

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta)\leq\ell\xi<\xi T,

establishing inequality (
7
).

Finally, note that if
G\sim SBM(n,k,B,\theta)
, we have
G_{j}\sim SBM(n,k,\frac{B}{T},\theta)
for each
j
. On the other hand, if we define
X_{j}=1\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{j}),\theta)\leq\xi\right\}
, the
X_{j}
’s may not be independent due to the fact that the thinned subgraphs all share the same underlying edges from
G
. Thus, we cannot apply a standard Chernoff bound (e.g., Lemma
14
). Nonetheless, we may bound the variance of
\sum_{j=1}^{T}X_{j}
and apply Chebyshev’s inequality. Indeed, we can consider the maximal correlation
\rho_{m}
(cf. Appendix
A.6
). For
\ell\neq m
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}(X_{\ell},X_{m})

\displaystyle\leq\rho(X_{\ell},X_{m})\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(X_{\ell})\operatorname{Var}(X_{m})}\leq\frac{\rho(X_{\ell},X_{m})}{4}\leq\frac{\rho_{m}(X_{\ell},X_{m})}{4}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\neq j}\frac{\rho_{m}(Y_{ij},Y_{ij}^{\prime})}{4},

where for each
i\neq j
, we define
Y_{ij}=Z_{ij}R_{ij}
and
Y_{ij}=Z_{ij}R^{\prime}_{ij}
, where
Z_{ij}\sim Ber(B_{ij})
and then we independently sample
R_{ij},R_{ij}^{\prime}\sim Ber\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
. By Lemma
30
, we have

\rho_{m}(Y_{ij},Y_{ij}^{\prime})=\frac{\frac{1}{T}(1-B_{ij})}{1-\frac{B_{ij}}{T}}.

In particular, for
n
sufficiently large, we have
\operatorname{Cov}(X_{\ell},X_{m})\leq\frac{1}{2T}
. It follows that

\operatorname{Var}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}X_{j}\right)=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{T}\operatorname{Var}(X_{j})+\sum_{\ell\neq m}\operatorname{Cov}(X_{\ell},X_{m})\right)\leq\frac{3}{4T},

so we can bound

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}X_{i}\leq\frac{1}{2}\right)\leq\frac{c}{T}=o(1),

as wanted.

D.2
Proof of Theorem
4

To establish privacy, we first show that
\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{(\epsilon/(4D))}
(i.e. Algorithm
5
applied with the symmetric edge-flip mechanism
\mathcal{M}_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D}}
) is
(\epsilon,0)_{2D}
–node DP.
Indeed, a result from Hehir et al.
[
35
]
immediately implies that
\mathcal{M}_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D}}
is
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4D},0\right)
-edge DP. Note that
[
35
]
proves
\mathcal{M}_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D}}
is
\frac{\epsilon}{4D}
-relationship DP (cf. Theorem
3.1
in
[
35
]
), but this notion implies
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4D},0\right)
-edge DP.

By group privacy for pure
(\epsilon,0)
-edge DP mechanisms,
\mathcal{M}_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D}}
is
(\epsilon,0)
-edge DP for
4D
edges that can change. When changing one node in graphs belonging to
\mathcal{G}_{n,2D}
, at most
4D
edges can be flipped. The desired privacy guarantee then follows by post-processing. Combined with Theorem
3
, we conclude that
\widehat{\theta}(G)
is
(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon,e^{\epsilon_{1}}\delta_{1})
-node DP, which is
(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon,O(\delta_{1}))
-node DP, since
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
and
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. This is the desired privacy guarantee.

For the utility guarantee, we begin by deriving a utility result with fixed privacy parameters:

Lemma 40

(Adapted from
[
35
]
)
.

Let
B_{0}\in(0,1]^{k\times k}
be invertible. Let
k\asymp 1
,
a_{n}\cdot\max(B_{0})\geq\frac{\log(n)}{n}
, and
\epsilon\in(0,\infty)
. Let
\lambda_{B_{0}}
be the smallest absolute non-zero eigenvalue of
B_{0}
. Let
G\in\mathcal{G}_{n}
be sampled from an SBM with
k
balanced communities and probability matrix
a_{n}B_{0}
. Let
\theta=\{\theta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the true community assignments, and let
\widehat{\theta}(G)=\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{(\epsilon)}(G)
be the output of Algorithm
5
with
\gamma\asymp 1
. Let

\displaystyle g_{\epsilon}=\frac{e^{\epsilon}+1}{e^{\epsilon}-1}\left(a_{n}\cdot\max(B_{0})+\frac{1}{e^{\epsilon}-1}\right).

Then there exist
c_{1},C_{1}\asymp 1
such that if
\frac{(2+\gamma)k^{3}ng_{\epsilon}}{n^{2}a_{n}^{2}\lambda_{B_{0}}^{2}}<c_{1}^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\epsilon}}{na_{n}^{2}},

with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{n}
.

Proof.

With the given assumptions, we have the existence of
c_{1},C_{1}\asymp 1
, by Theorem 5.1 from
[
35
]
. Because
\frac{(2+\gamma)k^{3}ng_{\epsilon}}{n^{2}a_{n}^{2}\lambda_{B_{0}}^{2}}<c_{1}^{-1}
, we have again by Theorem 5.1 from
[
35
]
that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{(\epsilon)}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{c_{1}(2+\gamma)k^{3}g_{\epsilon}}{na_{n}^{2}\lambda_{B_{0}}^{2}}\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\epsilon}}{na_{n}^{2}},

with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{n}
, because
k,\gamma,c_{1},\lambda_{B_{0}}\asymp 1
. This completes the proof.
∎

On the other hand, the privacy parameters used in the algorithm are
random
quantities
\frac{\epsilon}{4D\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G)}
. We first exploit the result from Lemma
28
to guarantee that
d_{T_{D}}(G)=0
, w.h.p., for
D\geq 3d
. Then
\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G)=\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},5+\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right\}
, which, w.h.p., lies in an interval
I
with bounds scaling as
\log(n)
. Finally, we will apply Lemma
40
with
\frac{\epsilon}{4Dx}
, for
x\in I
, to obtain the desired guarantee.

Let
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}:=5+\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
,
I:=\left[5+\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}},5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right]
, and
I_{max}:=5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
. For
F_{1}=\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\in I\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(F_{1}^{c}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)<-\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)>\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)\leq 2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}.

Let

\displaystyle g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D}}=\frac{e^{\frac{\epsilon}{4D}}+1}{e^{\frac{\epsilon}{4D}}-1}\left(a_{n}\cdot\max(B_{0})+\frac{1}{e^{\frac{\epsilon}{4D}}-1}\right).

Let
c_{1}
and
C_{1}
be as in Lemma
40
. Let
f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}
be the pdf of
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
, and let
\Omega_{0}
be as in the proof of Lemma
28
. We have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. In addition, on
\Omega_{0}
, we do not have to truncate the graph, since the degree of every node is less than
2d
, so
T_{D}(G)=G
and
d_{T_{D}}(G)=0
. Hence, since
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\geq 5>\frac{1}{2}
on
F_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{\left(\epsilon/(4D\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G))\right)}(T_{D}(G)),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{\left(\epsilon/(4D\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G))\right)}(T_{D}(G)),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\right\}\bigcap\Omega_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{\left(\epsilon/(4D\max\{1/2,X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\})\right)}(T_{D}(G)),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\right\}\bigcap\Omega_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{\left(\epsilon/(4DX_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}})\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\right\}\bigcap\Omega_{0}\cap F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{\left(\epsilon/(4DX_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}})\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\middle|X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{\left(\epsilon/(4Dx)\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{\left(\epsilon/(4Dx)\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4Dx}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since the function
g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4Dx}}
is increasing in
x>0
. We also dropped the conditioning on
\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right\}
, since the randomness in
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
only depends on the
\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)
noise, and this is independent of
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{(\epsilon/(4Dx))}(G),\theta\right)
. Assume
\frac{(2+\gamma)k^{3}ng_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{n^{2}a_{n}^{2}\lambda_{B_{0}}^{2}}<c_{1}^{-1}
(we will check this holds in both cases of the theorem hypothesis). Since
g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4Dx}}
is increasing in
x>0
, we have
\frac{(2+\gamma)k^{3}ng_{\frac{\epsilon}{4Dx}}}{n^{2}a_{n}^{2}\lambda_{B_{0}}^{2}}<c_{1}^{-1}
. Hence, by Lemma
40
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{EF}^{(\epsilon/(4Dx))}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4Dx}}}{na_{n}^{2}},

with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{n}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{n}-\mathbb{P}(F_{1}^{c})-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.
Now we split the analysis into two cases, and we check that
\frac{(2+\gamma)k^{3}ng_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{n^{2}a_{n}^{2}\lambda_{B_{0}}^{2}}<c_{1}^{-1}
in each case.

Take
\frac{D}{\sqrt{\log(n)}}<\epsilon<D
, and
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{2}(n)
. Since
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
and
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
, we have
I_{max}\asymp\log(n)
. Then
\frac{\epsilon}{DI_{max}}<\frac{1}{I_{max}}=o(1)
, and

\displaystyle g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}=\frac{e^{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}+1}{e^{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}-1}\left(a_{n}\cdot\max(B_{0})+\frac{1}{e^{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}-1}\right)\precsim\frac{D\log(n)}{\epsilon}\left(a_{n}+\frac{D\log(n)}{\epsilon}\right).

Therefore, since
\epsilon<D\leq n<n\log(n)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(2+\gamma)k^{3}ng_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{n^{2}a_{n}^{2}\lambda_{B_{0}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\precsim\frac{D\log(n)}{d\epsilon}+\frac{nD^{2}\log^{2}(n)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{nD^{2}\log^{2}(n)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}<\frac{D^{2}}{\log^{2}(n)\epsilon^{2}}<\frac{1}{\log(n)}<c_{1}^{-1},

for
n
large enough. So
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\precsim\frac{nD^{2}\log^{2}(n)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}<\frac{1}{\log(n)}
, with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

Now let
C^{\prime}
be such that
I_{max}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\log(n)}{4}
, and assume
C^{\prime}D\log^{2}(n)<\epsilon
. Then
g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}\precsim a_{n}+e^{-\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}
, so

\frac{(2+\gamma)k^{3}ng_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{n^{2}a_{n}^{2}\lambda_{B_{0}}^{2}}\asymp\frac{g_{\frac{\epsilon}{4D\cdot I_{max}}}}{na_{n}^{2}}\precsim\frac{1}{d}+\frac{n}{d^{2}e^{\epsilon/(C^{\prime}D\log(n))}}<\frac{1}{d}+\frac{1}{d^{2}}\precsim\frac{1}{d}<c_{1}^{-1},

for
n
large enough, since
d\succsim\log(n)
. So
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\precsim\frac{1}{d}\precsim\frac{1}{\log(n)}
, with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. This completes the proof.

D.3
Proofs for Section
5.4

D.3.1
Proof of Theorem
5

To establish privacy, we begin with the following result:

Lemma 41
.

Let
2\epsilon<\log(1/\delta)
. Then Algorithm
7
with privacy parameters
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4D},\delta\right)
is
(2\epsilon,\delta)_{2D}
-node DP.

Proof.

By Lemma
32
, we know that
\hat{A}_{(2k)}
(using privacy parameters
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4D},\delta\right)
) satisfies
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{64D\log(1/\delta)}
-edge zCDP. By postprocessing, Algorithm
7
satisfies
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{64D\log(1/\delta)}
-edge zCDP. When changing one node in graphs belonging to
\mathcal{G}_{n,2D}
, at most
4D
edges can be flipped. Hence, by group privacy (cf. Lemma
24
), Algorithm
7
satisfies
\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4\log(1/\delta)}\right)_{2D}
-zCDP. Finally, by Lemma
21
, we obtain
\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4\log(1/\delta)}+\epsilon,\delta\right)_{2D}
-DP. Since
\delta<e^{-2\epsilon}
, this is also
(9\epsilon/8,\delta)_{2D}
-DP, and the conclusion follows.
∎

To see the benefit of zCDP, suppose we used Lemma
32
and we converted it immediately to
(\epsilon,\delta)
-DP. We could use group privacy with parameters
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4D},\frac{\delta}{4D}\right)
after, to obtain a final guarantee of
(\epsilon,\delta e^{\epsilon})
-DP, provided
2\epsilon<\log(1/\delta)
. Hence, zCDP presents an improvement, especially when
\epsilon
grows quickly with
n
.

Thus, by Lemma
41
, we know that if
\frac{2\epsilon}{T}<\log(1/\delta)
, then
\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{(\epsilon/(4TD),\delta)}
is
(2\epsilon/T,\delta)_{2D}
-node DP. Since
\frac{2\epsilon}{T}<\log(1/(\delta\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)))
, Theorem
2
implies that
\widetilde{\theta}(G_{j})=\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{\left(\epsilon/(4TD\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G_{j})),\delta/\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G_{j})\right)}(T_{D}(G_{j}))
is
(\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon/T,1/\mathrm{poly}(n))
-node DP. We also used the fact that
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
and
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. Since
T\asymp\log(n)
, we know that
\epsilon/T
grows with
n
. Since
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
, we know that
\epsilon_{1}\leq\epsilon/T
, for
n
large enough, and thus,
\widetilde{\theta}(G_{j})
is
(3\epsilon/T,1/\mathrm{poly}(n))
-node DP. Finally, by Proposition
1
, we know that
\widehat{\theta}(G)
is
(3\epsilon,1/\mathrm{poly}(n))
-node DP.

For the utility guarantee, let us start by focusing on
G_{1}\sim SBM\left(n,k,\frac{B}{T}\right)
. We will then use Proposition
1
to boost the accuracy. Let
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}:=5+\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
,
I:=\left[5+\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}},5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right]
, and
I_{max}:=5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
. Then, for
F_{1}=\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\in I\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(F_{1}^{c}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)<-\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)>\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)\leq 2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}.

Let

\displaystyle h_{\epsilon,\delta}=\frac{T}{d}+\frac{nT^{2}\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{n^{2}T^{4}\log^{4}(n)\log^{2}(1/\delta)}{d^{4}\epsilon^{4}}.

Since we are working with
G_{1}\sim SBM\left(n,k,\frac{B}{T}\right)
, we need to scale
d
accordingly, as well.

We first derive the following result, proved in Appendix
D.3.2
, which bounds the accuracy of the algorithm with fixed privacy parameters
(\epsilon,\delta)
:

Lemma 42
.

Let
G\sim SBM(n,k,B,\theta)
, with parameters satisfying Assumption
1
. Let
\theta=\{\theta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the true community assignments, and let

\widehat{\theta}(G)=\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{(\epsilon,\delta)}(G)
be the output of Algorithm
7
with
\gamma\asymp 1
. Then there exists
c_{1}\asymp 1
such that if

(16+8\gamma)\left(\frac{1}{d}+\frac{n\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{n^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log^{2}(1/\delta)}{d^{4}\epsilon^{4}}\right)<c_{1},

we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\precsim\frac{1}{d}+\frac{n\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{n^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log^{2}(1/\delta)}{d^{4}\epsilon^{4}},

with probability at least
0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

In order to handle the random privacy parameters
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4TD\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G)},\frac{\delta}{\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G)}\right)
, we exploit the result from Lemma
28
to guarantee that w.h.p., for
D\geq\frac{3d}{T}
, we have
d_{T_{D}}(G_{1})=0
. Then note that
\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G_{1})=\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},5+\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right\}
, and w.h.p., we concentrate this in an interval
I
with bounds scaling as
\log(n)
. The utility based on
G_{1}
follows from Lemma
42
with
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}\right)
, for
x\in I
. The final utility guarantee for the boosted version applied to
G\sim SBM(n,k,B,\theta)
, follows from Proposition
1
.

To this end, let
c_{1}
and
C_{1}
be as in Lemma
42
. Next, let
f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}
be the pdf of
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
. Also, let
\Omega_{0}
be as in the proof of Lemma
28
applied to
\frac{a_{n}}{T}
. The guarantee on this event holds because we are under Assumption
1
applied to
\frac{a_{n}}{T}
. We have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. In addition, on
\Omega_{0}
, we do not have to truncate the graph, since the degree of every node is less than
\frac{2d}{T}
. So on
\Omega_{0}
, we have
T_{D}(G_{1})=G_{1}
and
d_{T_{D}}(G_{1})=0
. Hence, since
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\geq 5>\frac{1}{2}
on
F_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{\left(\epsilon/(4TD\max\{1/2,X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\}),\delta/\max\{1/2,X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\}\right)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\cap F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{\left(\epsilon/(4TDX_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}),\delta/X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\right)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\cap F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{\left(\epsilon/(4TDX_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}),\delta/X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\right)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\middle|X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{\left(\epsilon(4TDx),\delta/x\right)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{\left(\epsilon/(4TDx),\delta/x\right)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since the function
h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}}
is increasing in
x>0
. We also dropped the conditioning on
\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right\}
, since the randomness in
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
only depends on the
\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)
noise, and this is independent of
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{(\epsilon/(4TDx),\delta/x}(G_{1}),\theta\right)
. Now, assume
(16+8\gamma)h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}<c_{1}
(we will later check that this holds). Since
h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}}
is increasing in
x>0
, we have
(16+8\gamma)h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}}<c_{1}
. By Lemma
42
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{\left(\epsilon/(4TDx),\delta/x\right)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}},

with probability at least
0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\left(0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq 0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}-\mathbb{P}(F_{1}^{c})-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq 0.9-2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\geq 0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.
We now check that
(16+8\gamma)h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}<c_{1}
.

Recall that
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{5}(n)
and
\epsilon>D^{2}\log(n)
. Since
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
,
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
, we obtain
I_{max}\asymp\log(n)
. Since
\delta>\frac{e^{-\Theta(\epsilon/T)}}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
and
\frac{\epsilon}{T}
is growing with
n
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{D^{2}T^{4}n\log^{4}(n)\log(\log(n)/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{D^{2}T^{4}n\log^{5}(n)\epsilon}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}T}=\frac{D^{2}T^{3}n\log^{5}(n)}{d^{2}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{D^{2}n\log^{8}(n)}{d^{2}\epsilon}<\frac{D^{2}}{\epsilon\log^{2}(n)}<\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{T}{d}+\frac{D^{2}T^{4}I_{max}^{2}n\log^{2}(n)\log(I_{max}/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{D^{4}T^{8}I_{max}^{4}n^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log^{2}(I_{max}/\delta)}{d^{4}\epsilon^{4}}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{T}{d}+\frac{D^{2}T^{4}n\log^{4}(n)\log(\log(n)/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}.

Since
\gamma,c_{1}\asymp 1
, we obtain
(16+8\gamma)h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}<c_{1}
. Hence, since
T\asymp\log(n)
and
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{5}(n)
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\precsim\frac{T}{d}+\frac{D^{2}T^{4}n\log^{3}(n)\log(\log(n)/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}\precsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\log^{4}(n)}+\frac{D^{2}}{\epsilon\log^{2}(n)}\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)},

with probability at least
0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. So for
n
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\log^{3}(n)}+\frac{D^{2}}{\epsilon\log(n)}\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{2}(n)},

with probability at least
0.89
. Now we apply Algorithm
2
, i.e.,
\mathrm{GraphBoost}\left(T,\xi,G,\frac{3\epsilon}{T},\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},k,\widetilde{\theta}\right)
. By Proposition
1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\precsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\log^{2}(n)}+\frac{D^{2}}{\epsilon}\precsim\frac{1}{\log(n)},

with probability at least
1-\frac{c}{\log(n)}
, for some
0<c\asymp 1
. Since all the communities in
\theta
have size
\frac{n}{k}
, it is easy to see that
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq k\mathcal{L}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)
, and since
k\asymp 1
, the conclusion follows.

D.3.2
Proof of Lemma
42

Recall that
P=J(B\otimes J_{\frac{n}{k}})J^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, and let
\Pi\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be the projection onto
\text{col}(P)
. We run Algorithm
7
with adjacency matrix
A,k,\epsilon,\delta
, and
L\asymp\log(n)/\gamma_{1}
, where
\gamma_{1}=1-\frac{\sigma_{k+1}(A)}{\sigma_{k}(A)}
. By Lemma
32
, there exists an event
\Omega_{1}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1})\geq 0.9
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|A-\hat{A}_{(2k)}\right\|_{2}\leq\sigma_{k+1}(A)+O\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}(A)\sqrt{(n/\gamma_{1})\log(n)\log(\log(n)/\gamma_{1})}}{\sigma_{k}(A)-\sigma_{k+1}(A)}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon}\right),

on
\Omega_{1}
. By Lemma
27
, since
a_{n}\succsim\frac{\log(n)}{n}
, there is an event
\Omega_{2}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{2})\geq 1-\frac{1}{n}
, such that
||A-\mathbb{E}[A]||_{2}=||A-P+\text{diag}(P)||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{na_{n}}
on
\Omega_{2}
. Let us work on
\Omega_{2}^{\prime}=\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
. Hence,
||A-P||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{na_{n}}+a_{n}\precsim\sqrt{na_{n}}
. By Lemma
5
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}(A)=\sigma_{1}(A-P+P)\leq||A-P||_{2}+\sigma_{1}(P)\precsim\sqrt{na_{n}}+na_{n}\precsim na_{n}.

Also,

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(A)\geq\sigma_{k}(P)-||A-P||_{2}\succsim na_{n}-\sqrt{na_{n}}\succsim na_{n}.

Hence,
\sigma_{1}(A),\sigma_{k}(A)\asymp na_{n}
. Next, by Lemma
5
again, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{k+1}(A)\leq\sigma_{k+1}(P)+||A-P||_{2}=||A-P||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{na_{n}},

since
P
has rank
k
. So
\gamma_{1}\succsim 1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
. Hence,
\gamma_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, for
n
large enough, because
d\succsim\log(n)
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|A-\hat{A}_{(2k)}\right\|_{2}\precsim\sqrt{na_{n}}+\frac{\log(n)\sqrt{n\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon},

on
\Omega_{2}^{\prime}
. Write
\Pi=UU^{T}
, with
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
U^{T}U=I_{k}
. Let
\hat{\Pi}=U_{(k)}U_{(k)}^{T}
. Then

\displaystyle||\hat{\Pi}-\Pi||_{2}=||U_{(k)}U_{(k)}^{T}-UU^{T}||_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}2||\sin(\Theta)(U_{(k)},U)||_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}2||\sin(\Theta)(U_{(k)},U)||_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\mathop{\inf}_{Q:Q^{T}Q=QQ^{T}=I_{k}}||U_{(k)}Q-U||_{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}||U_{(k)}\hat{Q}-U||_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\precsim\frac{\sqrt{k}\left(\sigma_{1}(P)+\left\|\hat{A}_{(2k)}-P\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\hat{A}_{(2k)}-P\right\|_{2}}{\sigma_{k}^{2}(P)}\precsim\frac{\left(na_{n}+\left\|\hat{A}_{(2k)}-P\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\hat{A}_{(2k)}-P\right\|_{2}}{(na_{n})^{2}}.

Inequality (a) follows from Lemma
8
. Inequality (b) holds because the Frobenius norm is greater than the operator norm. Inequality (c) holds because of Lemma
11
. Inequality (d) and the existence of the
k\times k
orthogonal matrix
\hat{Q}
hold because of Lemma
10
. We also used the facts that
k\asymp 1
and
\sigma_{1}(P)\asymp\sigma_{k}(P)\asymp na_{n}
. Hence, since

\left\|\hat{A}_{(2k)}-P\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\hat{A}_{(2k)}-A\right\|_{2}+||A-P||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{na_{n}}+\frac{\log(n)\sqrt{n\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon},

we have with probability at least
0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
that

\displaystyle||\hat{\Pi}-\Pi||_{2}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{\left(na_{n}+\sqrt{na_{n}}+\frac{\log(n)\sqrt{n\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon}\right)\left(\sqrt{na_{n}}+\frac{\log(n)\sqrt{n\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon}\right)}{(na_{n})^{2}}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{\left(d+\frac{\log(n)\sqrt{n\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon}\right)\left(\sqrt{d}+\frac{\log(n)\sqrt{n\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon}\right)}{d^{2}}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}+\frac{\log(n)\sqrt{n\log(1/\delta)}}{d\epsilon}+\frac{n\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}.

Now note that by Lemma
8
and Lemma
11
, since
k\asymp 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\inf\limits_{Q\in\mathbb{V}_{k,k}}||U_{(k)}-UQ||_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}||U_{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}\leq||\sin(\Theta)(U_{(k)},U)||_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq k||\sin(\Theta)(U_{(k)},U)||_{2}^{2}\leq k||\hat{\Pi}-\Pi||_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{1}{d}+\frac{n\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{n^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log^{2}(1/\delta)}{d^{4}\epsilon^{4}},

where
Q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
and
Q^{\prime T}Q^{\prime}=I_{k}
. The rest of the proof follows the lines of argument from the proof of Theorem 5.1 in
[
35
]
. We know from
[
50
]
that
U=\Theta X
, for some
\Theta\in\mathbf{M}_{n\times k}
and
X
satisfying
||X_{j*}-X_{l*}||_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{2k}{n}}
, for
j\neq l
. Then
UQ^{\prime}=\Theta XQ^{\prime}=\Theta X^{\prime}
, with
X^{\prime}=XQ^{\prime}
, and

||X^{\prime}_{j*}-X^{\prime}_{l*}||_{2}=||(e_{j}-e_{l})^{T}XQ^{\prime}||_{2}=||X_{j*}-X_{l*}||_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{2k}{n}},

for
j\neq l
. Here,
e_{j}
is the
j^{\text{th}}
standard basis vector. For all
j\in[k]
, choose
\delta_{j}=\sqrt{\frac{2k}{n}}
and define
S_{j}
as in Lemma 5.3 of
[
50
]
. We want to show that
(16+8\gamma)||U_{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}<\frac{\delta_{j}^{2}n}{k}
, for all
j\in[k]
. Since
\frac{\delta_{j}^{2}n}{k}>1
, it is enough to prove that
(16+8\gamma)||U_{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}\leq 1
. Let
c_{1}\asymp 1
be the absolute constant such that

\displaystyle c_{1}||U_{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{d}+\frac{n\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{n^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log^{2}(1/\delta)}{d^{4}\epsilon^{4}}\right).

Then

\displaystyle(16+8\gamma)||U_{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{16+8\gamma}{c_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{d}+\frac{n\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{n^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log^{2}(1/\delta)}{d^{4}\epsilon^{4}}\right)\leq 1,

as desired. Thus, for each
community
j\in[k]
, the set of nodes that are possibly misclassified by Algorithm
7
must be a subset of
S_{j}
, and since
\frac{\delta_{j}^{2}n}{k}>1
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{ME}^{(\epsilon,\delta)}(G),\theta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\max}_{j\in[k]}\frac{k|S_{j}|}{n}\leq\frac{k}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{k}|S_{j}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}|S_{j}|\delta_{j}^{2}\leq(16+8\gamma)||U_{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16+8\gamma}{c_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{d}+\frac{n\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{n^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log^{2}(1/\delta)}{d^{4}\epsilon^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle=C_{1}\left(\frac{1}{d}+\frac{n\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}{d^{2}\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{n^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log^{2}(1/\delta)}{d^{4}\epsilon^{4}}\right),

where
C_{1}=\frac{16+8\gamma}{c_{1}}\asymp 1
, with probability at least
0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, as desired, since
\gamma,c_{1}\asymp 1
.

D.4
Proofs for Section
5.5
(unweighted case)

D.4.1
Proof of Theorem
6

Let
G_{1},\dots,G_{T}
be as in Algorithm
2
. Denote
\widetilde{\theta}(G_{j})=\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{\left(\epsilon/(5TD\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G_{j})),\delta/\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(G_{j})\right)}(T_{D}(G_{j}))
, for
j\in[T]
.

We start by analyzing the privacy of Algorithm
8
. The following result is proved in Appendix
D.4.2
:

Lemma 43
.

Let
2\epsilon<\log(1/\delta)
. Then Algorithm
8
with privacy parameters
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{5D},\delta\right)
is
(2\epsilon,\delta)_{2D}
-node DP (for both
\mathcal{G}_{n,2D}
and
\mathcal{W}_{n,2D}
).

By Lemma
43
, we know that if
\frac{2\epsilon}{T}<\log(1/\delta)
, then
\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5TD),\delta)}
is
(2\epsilon/T,\delta)_{2D}
-node DP. Since
\frac{2\epsilon}{T}<\log(1/(\delta\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)))
, Theorem
2
implies that
\widetilde{\theta}(G_{j})
is
(\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon/T,1/\mathrm{poly}(n))
-node DP. We also used the fact that
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
and
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. Since
T\asymp\log(n)
, we know that
\epsilon/T
grows with
n
. So since
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
, we have
\epsilon_{1}\leq\epsilon/T
for
n
sufficiently large, implying that
\widetilde{\theta}(G_{j})
is
(3\epsilon/T,1/\mathrm{poly}(n))
-node DP. Finally, by Proposition
1
, we know that
\widehat{\theta}(G)
is
(3\epsilon,1/\mathrm{poly}(n))
-node DP.

For the utility guarantee, we first focus on
G_{1}\sim SBM\left(n,k,\frac{B}{T}\right)
. We will then use Proposition
1
. The following result, proved in Appendix
D.4.3
, provides a utility guarantee for deterministic privacy parameters:

Lemma 44
.

Assume
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\log(1/\delta)}\geq\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}
and
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{11/2}(n)
. Suppose Assumption
4
and equation (
5
) hold. Let
\theta=\{\theta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the true community assignments. Let
\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon,\delta)}(G)
be the output of Algorithm
8
. Then there exists an absolute constant
C_{1}\asymp 1
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon,\delta)}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon\log^{4}(n)}\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{5/2}(n)},

with probability at least
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

Let
1\asymp\zeta\in(0,1/3)
. Let
I:=\left[5+\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}},5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right]
,
I_{min}:=5+\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
, and
I_{max}:=5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
. Let
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}:=5+\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
. Then for
F_{1}=\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\in I\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(F_{1}^{c}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)<-\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)>\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)\leq 2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}.

Let
h_{\epsilon,\delta}=\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon\log^{4}(n)}
and let
C_{1}
be as in Lemma
44
. Let
f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}
be the pdf of
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
. Also, let
\Omega_{0}
be as in the proof of Lemma
28
applied to
\frac{a_{n}}{T}
. The guarantee on this event holds because we are under Assumption
1
applied to
\frac{a_{n}}{T}
. We have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. In addition, on
\Omega_{0}
, we do not have to truncate the graph, since the degree of every node is less than
\frac{2d}{T}
. Hence, on
\Omega_{0}
, we have
T_{D}(G_{1})=G_{1}
and
d_{T_{D}}(G_{1})=0
. Since
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\geq 5>\frac{1}{2}
on
F_{1}
, we then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5TD\max\{1/2,X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\}),\delta/\max\{1/2,X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\})}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\cap F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5TDX_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}),\delta/X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}})}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\cap F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5TDX_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}),\delta/X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}})}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\middle|X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5TDx),\delta/x)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5TDx),\delta/x)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since the function
h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}}
is increasing in
x>0
. We also dropped the conditioning on
\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right\}
, since the randomness in
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
only depends on the
\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)
noise, and this is independent of
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5TDx),\delta/x)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)
. We now use Lemma
44
, with
\frac{\epsilon}{5TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}
, and
x\in[I_{min},I_{max}]
. For this, we need
d>\sqrt{n}T\log^{11/2}(n)
, and

\displaystyle\max\left\{(C^{(1)})^{2}n^{1-2\beta_{0}}\log(n),\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}\right\}<\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{25D^{2}T^{2}x^{2}\log(x/\delta)}\leq\frac{(C^{(1)})^{2}n\log(n)}{4}

(Assumption
4
with
\frac{\epsilon}{5DTx}
), which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}<\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{25D^{2}T^{2}x^{2}\log(x/\delta)}\leq\frac{(C^{(1)})^{2}n\log(n)}{4},

since
\beta_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
, so for
n
large enough, we have
(C^{(1)})^{2}n^{1-2\beta_{0}}\log(n)\leq\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}
. We already have
d>\sqrt{n}T\log^{11/2}(n)
, by assumption. Next, since
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
and
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, we have
I_{max},I_{min}\asymp\log(n)
. Since
\frac{2\epsilon}{T}<\log(1/\delta)
, and
T\asymp\log(n)
, we then obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}T^{2}x^{2}\log(x/\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}T^{2}I_{min}^{2}\log(I_{min}/\delta)}\asymp\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log(\log(n)/\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log(1/\delta)}\precsim\frac{\epsilon}{D^{2}\log^{3}(n)}<n.

Hence, for
n
large enough, we have
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{25D^{2}T^{2}x^{2}\log(x/\delta)}\leq\frac{(C^{(1)})^{2}n\log(n)}{4}
. Now, since
T\log(1/(\delta\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)))<\Theta(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}T^{2}x^{2}\log(x/\delta)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}T^{2}I_{max}^{2}\log(I_{max}/\delta)}\asymp\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}\log^{4}(n)\log(\log(n)/\delta)}

\displaystyle\succsim\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}\log^{4}(n)(\log(n)+\epsilon/T)}\succsim\frac{\epsilon}{D^{2}\log^{3}(n)}>\frac{1}{\log^{2}(n)},

since
\frac{\epsilon}{T}
grows larger than
\log(n)
. So for
n
large enough, we have
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{25D^{2}T^{2}x^{2}\log(x/\delta)}\geq\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}
. Hence, we can use Lemma
44
, implying that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5TDx),\delta/x)}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDx},\frac{\delta}{x}},

with probability at least
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5TDI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\int_{I}\left(0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq 0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}-\mathbb{P}(F_{1}^{c})-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq 0.9-3\zeta-2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\geq 0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. Hence, since
I_{max}\asymp\log(n)
,
D^{2}\log(n)<\epsilon
, and
T\log(1/(\delta\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)))<\Theta(\epsilon)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{DTI_{max}\sqrt{\log(I_{max}/\delta)}}{\epsilon\log^{4}(n)}\precsim\frac{D\log^{2}(n)\sqrt{\log(n)+\epsilon/T}}{\epsilon\log^{4}(n)}\precsim\frac{D}{\log^{5/2}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}<\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)},

with probability at least
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. For a suitable choice of
\zeta\in(0,1/3)
, we obtain
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\geq 0.87
, for
n
large enough, implying that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widetilde{\theta}(G_{1}),\theta\right)\leq\frac{D}{\log^{3/2}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}<\frac{1}{\log^{2}(n)},

with probability at least
0.87
. Now we apply Algorithm
2
, i.e.,
\mathrm{GraphBoost}\left(T,\xi,G,\frac{3\epsilon}{T},\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},k,\widetilde{\theta}\right)
. By Proposition
1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\precsim\frac{D}{\log^{1/2}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}<\frac{1}{\log(n)},

with probability at least
1-\frac{c}{\log(n)}
, for some
0<c\asymp 1
. Since all the communities in
\theta
have size
\frac{n}{k}
, it is easy to see that
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq k\mathcal{L}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)
, and since
k\asymp 1
, the conclusion follows.

D.4.2
Proof of Lemma
43

The proofs for the unweighted and weighted graphs are similar, so we discuss both simultaneously. There are two privatization steps in the algorithm: The application of GoodCenter
q
times and the Gaussian noise addition step, also happening
q
times. Let us examine each in turn.

By Lemma
31
, one application of GoodCenter in Algorithm
8
is
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{800D^{2}q\log(1/\delta)}
-zCDP, when changing one of the
t
points. When changing one edge in the graph (either flip or weight-change), at most
2
of the
t
points can change. By the composition of zCDP mechanisms, i.e., Lemma
22
, one application of GoodCenter in Algorithm
8
is
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{200D^{2}q\log(1/\delta)}
-edge zCDP. Since there are
q
applications, we obtain
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{200D^{2}\log(1/\delta)}
-edge zCDP, by Lemma
22
.

Next, recall that when changing one edge, we change at most
2
of the
t
chunks. Thus, the
\ell_{2}
-sensitivity of the average of
t
points is
\frac{4r}{t}
. Using Lemma
25
, one application of the Gaussian mechanism in Algorithm
8
is
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{200D^{2}q\log(1/\delta)}
-edge zCDP. Since there are
q
applications, we obtain
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{200D^{2}\log(1/\delta)}
-edge zCDP, by Lemma
22
.

By Lemma
22
again, we see that Algorithm
8
is
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{100D^{2}\log(1/\delta)}
-edge zCDP. When changing one node, provided all changes remain in
\mathcal{G}_{n,2D}
or
\mathcal{W}_{n,2D}
, at most
4D
connections from that node are changed. For unweighted graphs, there can only be flips, while in the weighted case, we can set edges to
0
, set them to some non-zero weight, or set their non-zero weight to another non-zero value. In either case, at most
4D+1
of the
t
chunks are changed, so at most
5D
(for
n
large enough) chunks can change. Hence, by group privacy, using Lemma
24
, Algorithm
8
with privacy parameters
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{5D},\delta\right)
is
\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4\log(1/\delta)}\right)_{2D}
-zCDP. Finally, by Lemma
21
, we obtain
\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4\log(1/\delta)}+\epsilon,\delta\right)_{2D}
-DP. Since
\delta<e^{-2\epsilon}
, this is also
(9\epsilon/8,\delta)_{2D}
-DP, and the conclusion follows.

D.4.3
Proof of Lemma
44

The following result, proved in Appendix
D.4.4
, shows that w.h.p., when dividing
P=[P_{1}^{T},\dots,P_{t}^{T}]^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
according to how
A
is divided in step
1
, all chunks have rank
k
:

Lemma 45
.

Let
\Omega_{1}
be the event that
P_{1},\dots,P_{t}
all have rank
k
. Then

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1})\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}.

From now on, we work in the event
\Omega_{1}
. Since each chunk has rank
k
, the projection onto
\text{row}(P_{j})
is
\Pi
, for all
j\in[t]
. Let
P=UD_{P}U^{T}
, with
D_{P}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
diagonal and
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
having orthonormal columns. Then
\Pi=UU^{T}
.

The following result is proved in Appendix
D.4.7
:

Lemma 46
.

Let
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{11/2}(n)
. Let
E:=E_{Z}+E_{Z^{\perp}}
, i.e., the decomposition of each column of
E
as a sum of the component in the column span of
Z
and the corresponding orthogonal complement. Let
Z^{\prime}=Z+E_{Z}
. Assume
\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}=o(1)
. Then
\sigma_{k}(Z^{\prime})\asymp\sqrt{k}\asymp 1
, and
\Pi
is also the projection onto the columns of
Z^{\prime}
, with probability at least
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

Since
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\log(1/\delta)}\geq\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}
, we have

\displaystyle\xi_{0}=\frac{\log^{1/4}(1/\delta)}{\log^{5/2}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}+\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\log^{5}(n)\epsilon}\asymp\frac{\log^{1/4}(1/\delta)}{\log^{5/2}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}\leq\frac{1}{\log^{7/4}(n)},

so,
\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{5/4}(n)}=o(1)
. Hence, we can use Lemma
46
.

Consider the SVD of
\hat{Z}=\hat{U}\hat{D}\hat{V}^{T}
and also the SVD of
Z^{\prime}=U^{\prime}D^{\prime}V^{\prime T}
. Write
U^{\prime}=\left[U^{\prime(k)},U^{\prime(k)}_{\perp}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
and
V^{\prime}=\left[V^{\prime(k)},V^{\prime(k)}_{\perp}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times q}
. Then

\displaystyle a_{1}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{min}\left((U^{\prime(k)})^{T}\hat{Z}V^{\prime(k)}\right)=\sigma_{min}\left((U^{\prime(k)})^{T}Z^{\prime}V^{\prime(k)}+(U^{\prime(k)})^{T}E_{Z^{\perp}}V^{\prime(k)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{min}\left((U^{\prime(k)})^{T}Z^{\prime}V^{\prime(k)}\right)=\sigma_{k}(Z^{\prime})\asymp\sqrt{k}\asymp 1.

This holds since, by Lemma
46
, the columns of
U^{\prime(k)}
are in the span of
Z
, so they are orthogonal to the columns of
E_{Z^{\perp}}
. Now, recall that, under Assumption
4
, we have
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\log(1/\delta)}\precsim n\log(n)
, so
\frac{\log^{1/4}(1/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\succsim\frac{\log^{1/4}(n)}{n^{1/4}}\succsim\frac{1}{n^{2}\sqrt{n}}
. Hence, we have
\frac{1}{n^{2}\sqrt{n}}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}\asymp\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}
.

The following result is proved in Appendix
D.4.6
:

Lemma 47
.

Let
Z=[\Pi z_{1},\dots,\Pi z_{q}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times q}
, and suppose
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{11/2}(n)
. We have
\|\hat{Z}-Z\|_{2}\precsim\frac{1}{n^{2}\sqrt{n}}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}
, with probability at least
0.9-2\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

Using Lemma
47
, we obtain

\displaystyle b_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\|(U^{\prime(k)}_{\perp})^{T}\hat{Z}V^{\prime(k)}_{\perp}\right\|_{2}=\left\|(U^{\prime(k)}_{\perp})^{T}Z^{\prime}V^{\prime(k)}_{\perp}+(U^{\prime(k)}_{\perp})^{T}E_{Z^{\perp}}V^{\prime(k)}_{\perp}\right\|_{2}=\left\|(U^{\prime(k)}_{\perp})^{T}E_{Z^{\perp}}V^{\prime(k)}_{\perp}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq||E_{Z^{\perp}}||_{2}\leq||E||_{2}\precsim\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)},

since the columns of
U^{\prime(k)}_{\perp}
are orthogonal to the columns of
Z^{\prime}
. Next, for a matrix
M
, let
\Pi_{M}
be the projection matrix onto the columns of
M
. Then

\displaystyle z_{12}=\left\|\Pi_{U^{\prime(k)}}E_{Z^{\perp}}\Pi_{V^{\prime(k)}_{\perp}}\right\|_{2}=0,

since the columns of
U^{\prime(k)}
are orthogonal to the columns of
E_{Z^{\perp}}
, using Lemma
46
. Also, by Lemma
47
, again, we have

\displaystyle z_{21}=\left\|\Pi_{U^{\prime(k)}_{\perp}}E_{Z^{\perp}}\Pi_{V^{\prime(k)}}\right\|_{2}\leq||E_{Z^{\perp}}||_{2}\leq||E||_{2}\precsim\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}.

With
a_{1},b_{1},z_{12}
, and
z_{21}
in mind, by Lemma
8
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sin(\Theta)(U^{\prime(k)},\hat{U}^{(k)})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{a_{1}z_{21}+b_{1}z_{12}}{a_{1}^{2}-b_{1}^{2}-\min\{z_{12}^{2},z_{21}^{2}\}}=\frac{a_{1}z_{21}}{a_{1}^{2}-b_{1}^{2}}\precsim\frac{b_{1}}{1-b_{1}^{2}}.

Hence, by Lemma
8
, and because the column span of
Z^{\prime}
is the same as the column span of
P
(both having rank
k
by Lemma
46
), we have

\displaystyle||\hat{\Pi}-\Pi||_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\hat{U}^{(k)}(\hat{U}^{(k)})^{T}-U^{\prime(k)}(U^{\prime(k)})^{T}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4\left\|\sin(\Theta)(U^{\prime(k)},\hat{U}^{(k)})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{b_{1}^{2}}{(1-b_{1}^{2})^{2}}\precsim\xi_{0}^{2}\log(n)\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{5/2}(n)},

since
b_{1}=o(1)
. Now note that by Lemma
8
and Lemma
11
, since
k\asymp 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\inf\limits_{Q\in\mathbb{V}_{k,k}}||\hat{U}^{(k)}-UQ||_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}||\hat{U}^{(k)}-UQ||_{F}^{2}\leq||\sin(\Theta)(\hat{U}^{(k)},U)||_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq k||\sin(\Theta)(\hat{U}^{(k)},U)||_{2}^{2}\leq k||\hat{\Pi}-\Pi||_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\precsim\xi_{0}^{2}\log(n),

where
Q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
and
Q^{\prime T}Q^{\prime}=I_{k}
. The rest of the proof follows the argument from the proof of Theorem 5.1 in
[
35
]
. We know from
[
50
]
that
U=\Theta X
, for some
\Theta\in\mathbf{M}_{n\times k}
and
X
satisfying
||X_{j*}-X_{l*}||_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{2k}{n}}
, for
j\neq l
. Then
UQ^{\prime}=\Theta XQ^{\prime}=\Theta X^{\prime}
, with
X^{\prime}=XQ^{\prime}
and

||X^{\prime}_{j*}-X^{\prime}_{l*}||_{2}=||(e_{j}-e_{l})^{T}XQ^{\prime}||_{2}=||X_{j*}-X_{l*}||_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{2k}{n}},

for
j\neq l
. Here,
e_{j}
is the
j^{\text{th}}
standard basis vector. For all
j\in[k]
, choose
\delta_{j}=\sqrt{\frac{2k}{n}}
and define
S_{j}
as in Lemma 5.3 of
[
50
]
. We want to show that
(16+8\gamma)||\hat{U}^{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}<\frac{\delta_{j}^{2}n}{k}
, for all
j\in[k]
. Since
\frac{\delta_{j}^{2}n}{k}>1
, it is enough to prove
(16+8\gamma)||\hat{U}^{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}\leq 1
. Let
c_{1}\asymp 1
be the absolute constant such that

\displaystyle c_{1}||\hat{U}^{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}\leq\xi_{0}^{2}\log(n).

Then

\displaystyle(16+8\gamma)||\hat{U}^{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{(16+8\gamma)\xi_{0}^{2}\log(n)}{c_{1}}\leq 1,

as desired, since
\xi_{0}^{2}\log(n)=o(1)
and
\gamma,c_{1}\asymp 1
. Thus, for each
community
j\in[k]
, the set of nodes that are possibly misclassified by Algorithm
8
must be a subset of
S_{j}
, and since
\frac{\delta_{j}^{2}n}{k}>1
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon,\delta)}(G),\theta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\max}_{j\in[k]}\frac{k|S_{j}|}{n}\leq\frac{k}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{k}|S_{j}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}|S_{j}|\delta_{j}^{2}\leq(16+8\gamma)||\hat{U}^{(k)}-UQ^{\prime}||_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(16+8\gamma)\xi_{0}^{2}\log(n)}{c_{1}}\leq\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon\log^{4}(n)}\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{5/2}(n)},

for
C_{1}=\frac{(16+8\gamma)}{c_{1}}\asymp 1
, with probability at least
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, as desired, since
\gamma,c_{1}\asymp 1
.

D.4.4
Proof of Lemma
45

Let
G_{j}=\{P_{j}\text{ has rank }k\}
. Then
\mathbb{P}(G_{1})=\dots=\mathbb{P}(G_{t})
, and

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(G_{1})=\mathbb{P}(\text{all of the distinct types of rows in }P\text{ appear at least once in chunk }1)

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}(\cup_{j=1}^{k}M_{j}^{c}),

where

M_{j}=\{\text{row of type }j\text{ in }P\text{ appears at least once}\}.

For
L\subseteq[k]
, let

K_{L}=\{\text{none of the types in }L\text{ appear at all}\}.

By the inclusion-exclusion principle, we have

\displaystyle p_{1}=1-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}(-1)^{\ell-1}\sum_{|L|=\ell}\mathbb{P}(K_{L}).

We first show that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1})\geq 1-t(1-p_{1})
. Note that
K_{L}=\emptyset
for
\ell=k
. There are
\binom{k}{\ell}
ways of choosing the set
L
from
[k]
such that
|L|=\ell
. Out of the
\binom{n}{m}
ways choose the rows of chunk 1, there are
\binom{n-\frac{\ell n}{k}}{m}
ways such that none of the distinct types in
L
appear at all, implying that

\displaystyle p_{1}=1-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{\ell-1}\binom{k}{\ell}\frac{\binom{n-\frac{\ell n}{k}}{m}}{\binom{n}{m}}.

Clearly, we then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1})\geq 1-\sum_{j=1}^{t}\mathbb{P}(G_{j}^{c})\geq 1-t(1-p_{1}),

as claimed.

Now recall that
k,\zeta\asymp 1
and
t\leq n^{\beta_{0}}
, so
m\geq n^{1-\beta_{0}}
. Observe that for
\ell\in\{1,\dots,k-1\}
and
g_{0}=\frac{\ell}{k}\asymp 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\binom{n-g_{0}n}{m}}{\binom{n}{m}}=\prod_{i=1}^{g_{0}n}\frac{n-m-g_{0}n+i}{n-g_{0}n+i}=\prod_{i=1}^{g_{0}n}\left(1-\frac{m}{n-g_{0}n+i}\right).

Then

\displaystyle\Omega\left(3^{-\frac{g_{0}m}{1-g_{0}}}\right)=\left(1-\frac{m}{n-g_{0}n}\right)^{g_{0}n}\leq\frac{\binom{n-g_{0}n}{m}}{\binom{n}{m}}\leq\left(1-\frac{m}{n}\right)^{g_{0}n}=O\left(2^{-g_{0}m}\right).

With the given scaling of
k,t
, and
m
in terms of
n
, we have
-\log\left(\frac{\binom{n-g_{0}n}{m}}{\binom{n}{m}}\right)\asymp m\succsim n^{1-\beta_{0}}
. Thus,
1-p_{1}
is a sum of
k-1\asymp 1
terms that decay exponentially to
0
, with exponent
\Omega(n^{1-\beta_{0}})
. Since
t\leq n^{\beta_{0}}
and
\beta_{0}\in(1/2,1)
, we have
t(1-p_{1})\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

D.4.5
Proof of Lemma
49

Recall that
z_{i}\sim N(0,I_{n})
. Fix
j\in[t]
. By Lemma
45
, the projection onto the row space of
P_{j}
is
\Pi
. We have

\displaystyle||\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi||_{2}

\displaystyle=||U^{(j),(k)}(U^{(j),(k)})^{T}-UU^{T}||_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}2||\sin(\Theta)(U^{(j),(k)},U)||_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}2||\sin(\Theta)(U^{(j),(k)},U)||_{F}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\mathop{\inf}_{Q:Q^{T}Q=QQ^{T}=I_{k}}||U^{(j),(k)}Q-U||_{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}||U^{(j),(k)}\hat{Q}-U||_{F}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{(\sigma_{1}(P_{j})+||\Pi_{j}A_{j}-P_{j}||_{2})||\Pi_{j}A_{j}-P_{j}||_{2}}{\sigma_{k}^{2}(P_{j})},

where we write
\sigma_{1}(\cdot)
and
\sigma_{k}(\cdot)
to denote the largest and
k^{\text{th}}
largest singular values, respectively. Inequality (a) follows from Lemma
8
. Inequality (b) holds because the Frobenius norm is greater than the operator norm. Inequality (c) holds because of Lemma
11
. Inequality (d) and the existence of the
k\times k
orthogonal matrix
\hat{Q}
hold because of Lemma
10
. Note that
\Pi_{j}A_{j}
is the best rank-
k
approximation of
A_{j}
in the operator norm, by Lemma
4
. Since
P_{j}
has rank
k
, we have
||\Pi_{j}A_{j}-A_{j}||_{2}\leq||A_{j}-P_{j}||_{2}\leq||A-P||_{2}
, further implying that

\displaystyle||\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi||_{2}\precsim\frac{(\sigma_{1}(P_{j})+||A-P||_{2})||A-P||_{2}}{\sigma_{k}^{2}(P_{j})}.

Thus, by Lemma
27
, since
a_{n}\succsim\frac{\log(n)}{n}
, there is an event
\Omega_{2}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{2})\geq 1-\frac{1}{n}
, such that

||A-\mathbb{E}[A]||_{2}=||A-P+\text{diag}(P)||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{na_{n}}

on
\Omega_{2}
. Let us work on
\Omega_{2}^{\prime}=\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
. Then

\displaystyle||\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi||_{2}\precsim\frac{\sqrt{k}(\sigma_{1}(P_{j})+\sqrt{na_{n}}+a_{n})(\sqrt{na_{n}}+a_{n})}{\sigma_{k}^{2}(P_{j})}.

We now have the following result, proved in Appendix
D.4.8
:

Lemma 48
.

Suppose
m\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and
P_{m}
is a matrix obtained by randomly subsampling
m
rows of
P
without replacement, i.e.,
P_{m}=E_{m}P
, where
E_{m}\in\{0,1\}^{m\times n}
(
E_{m}
selects a uniformly random subset of rows, of size
m
). Suppose
k\asymp 1
.
With probability at least
1-2ke^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(P_{m})

\displaystyle\succsim\sigma_{k}(D_{P})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}(P_{m})

\displaystyle\precsim\sigma_{\max}(D_{P})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}},

where
P=UD_{P}U^{T}
is the spectral decomposition of
P
, with
D_{P}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
.

By Lemma
48
, there is an event
\Omega_{3}
, with probability at least
1-2kte^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}
(a union bound over all
j\in[t]
), such that we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(P)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}\precsim\sigma_{k}(P_{j})\leq\sigma_{1}(P_{j})

\displaystyle\precsim\sigma_{1}(P)\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}.

Let us work on
\Omega_{3}^{\prime}=\Omega_{3}\cap\Omega_{2}^{\prime}
. Recall that
P=J(B\otimes J_{\frac{n}{k}})J^{T}
, where
J
is a permutation matrix. Then
\sigma_{1}(P)=\frac{na_{n}}{k}\sigma_{1}(B_{0})
and
\sigma_{k}(P)=\frac{na_{n}}{k}\sigma_{k}(B_{0})>0
. Since
B_{0}
does not change with
n
, we then have

\displaystyle a_{n}\sqrt{mn}\precsim\sigma_{k}(P_{j})\leq\sigma_{1}(P_{j})

\displaystyle\precsim a_{n}\sqrt{mn}.

Therefore, since
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{11/2}(n)
, we have

\displaystyle||\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi||_{2}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{(a_{n}\sqrt{mn}+\sqrt{na_{n}}+a_{n})(\sqrt{na_{n}}+a_{n})}{a_{n}^{2}mn}\precsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}m}}+\frac{1}{a_{n}m}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{t}{d}}+\frac{t}{d}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{5/2}(n)\rho^{1/4}}+\frac{1}{\log^{5}(n)\sqrt{\rho}}\asymp\frac{\log^{1/4}(1/\delta)}{\log^{5/2}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}+\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\log^{5}(n)\epsilon}=\xi_{0},

for all
j\in[t]
. Now we follow the argument in Corollary 4.6 in Singhal & Steinke
[
64
]
.
Let
\hat{\Pi}^{(j)}
be the projection matrix for the union of the subspace spanned by the columns of
U^{(j),(k)}
and the subspace spanned
by the columns of
U
. By Lemma
13
, we have
||\hat{\Pi}^{(j)}z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{\log(qt)}\precsim\sqrt{\log(n)}
, for all
(i,j)
, on an event
\Omega_{41}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{41})\geq 0.99
. Again by Lemma
13
we have
||\Pi z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{\log(qt)}\precsim\sqrt{\log(n)}
, for all
(i,j)
, on an event
\Omega_{42}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{42})\geq 0.99
. We also have, by Lemma
13
, that
||\Pi^{(j)}z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{\log(qt)}\precsim\sqrt{\log(n)}
, for all
(i,j)
, on an event
\Omega_{43}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{43})\geq 0.99
. Let us work on
\Omega_{4}^{\prime}=\Omega_{41}\cap\Omega_{42}\cap\Omega_{43}\cap\Omega_{3}^{\prime}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle||(\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi)z_{i}||_{2}=||(\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi)\hat{\Pi}^{(j)}z_{i}||_{2}\leq||\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi||_{2}||\hat{\Pi}^{(j)}z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)},

with probability at least
0.9-\tau(n)
, where
\tau(n)\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}+\frac{1}{n}+2kte^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}
. Recall that we assumed
m\geq n^{1-\beta_{0}}
and
1\asymp\beta_{0}\in(1/2,1)
, so
2kte^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}\leq 2kn^{\beta_{0}}e^{-\frac{n^{1-\beta_{0}}}{2k^{2}}}
. For
\zeta,k\asymp 1
as in Lemma
45
, we have
\tau(n)\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

D.4.6
Proof of Lemma
47

Define
\bar{Z}:=\left[\frac{1}{t}\sum_{j=1}^{t}z_{1}^{j},\dots,\frac{1}{t}\sum_{j=1}^{t}z_{q}^{j}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times q}
,

\displaystyle E_{0}

\displaystyle:=\bar{Z}-Z,

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle:=\hat{Z}-\bar{Z},

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:=E_{0}+E_{1}=\hat{Z}-Z.

We analyze GoodCenter and argue that, with high probability, no truncation is necessary. First, the randomness on
\Omega_{4}^{\prime}
is independent from the randomness for privatizing
\mathrm{GoodCenter}_{X_{i},0,R_{max},\frac{r_{min}}{2},\frac{\zeta}{q},\rho}
, for every
i\in[q]
. Hence, we argue conditionally on
\Omega_{4}^{\prime}
. Fix
i\in[q]
. Let
z_{i}^{grid}
be the closest point on the grid
\left[-\frac{R_{max}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{R_{max}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{n}=\left[-\log(n),\log(n)\right]^{n}
, with spacing
r_{min}
, to
\Pi z_{i}
. The following result is proved in Appendix
D.4.5
:

Lemma 49
.

Let
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{11/2}(n)
. For all
i\in[q]
and
j\in[t]
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi^{(j)}z_{i}\right\|_{2},||\Pi z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{\log(n)},

\displaystyle\|(\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi)z_{i}\|_{2}\precsim\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)},

with probability at least
0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, where

\displaystyle\xi_{0}:=\frac{\log^{1/4}(1/\delta)}{\log^{5/2}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}+\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\log^{5}(n)\epsilon}.

First, we know that
\{z_{i}^{j,grid}\}_{j=1}^{t}
, and
z_{i}^{grid}
lie on the grid
\left[-\frac{R_{max}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{R_{max}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{n}
with spacing
r_{min}
. By Lemma
49
, we have
||z_{i}^{j}||_{2},||\Pi z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{\log(n)}
and
||z_{i}^{j}-\Pi z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}
, for all
j\in[t]
. So for
n
large enough,
\{z_{i}^{j}\}_{j=1}^{t}
and
\Pi z_{i}
lie inside the surface enclosed by the grid
\left[-\frac{R_{max}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{R_{max}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]^{n}
. Since
z_{i}^{j,grid}
, and
z_{i}^{grid}
are the closest points on the grid to
z_{i}^{j}
, and
\Pi z_{i}
, respectively, and since
z_{i}^{j,grid},z_{i}^{grid}
lie on the grid themselves, we obtain, for all
j\in[t]
, that
||z_{i}^{j}-z_{i}^{j,grid}||_{2},||\Pi z_{i}-z_{i}^{grid}||_{2}\precsim r_{min}\sqrt{n}
. Using Lemma
49
, we have

\displaystyle||z_{i}^{j,grid}-z_{i}^{grid}||_{2},||z_{i}^{j,grid}-z_{i}^{l,grid}||_{2}\precsim r_{min}\sqrt{n}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)},

(8)

for all
j,l\in[t]
. Next, for our choice of
t
in equation (
5
), we obtain from Lemma
31
that with probability at least
1-\zeta/q
, we have
||z_{i}^{j,grid}-\theta_{i}^{*}||_{2}\leq 7r_{opt}(X_{i}^{\prime})
, for all
z_{i}^{j,grid}\in X_{i}^{\prime}
, where
X_{i}^{\prime}
is the subset with
|X_{i}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{t}{2}
. Hence, since
X_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq X_{i}
, we have, using inequality (
8
), that
||z_{i}^{j,grid}-\theta_{i}^{*}||_{2}\precsim r_{min}\sqrt{n}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}
. Now, for
z_{i}^{j,grid}\notin X_{i}^{\prime}
, we have

||z_{i}^{j,grid}-\theta_{i}^{*}||_{2}\leq||z_{i}^{j,grid}-z_{i}^{j^{\prime},grid}||_{2}+||z_{i}^{j^{\prime},grid}-\theta_{i}^{*}||_{2}\precsim r_{min}\sqrt{n}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)},

where
z_{i}^{j^{\prime},grid}
is any point in
X_{i}^{\prime}
. Therefore,
||z_{i}^{j,grid}-\theta_{i}^{*}||_{2}\precsim r_{min}\sqrt{n}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}
, for all
j\in[t]
, so

||z_{i}^{j}-\theta_{i}^{*}||_{2}\leq||z_{i}^{j,grid}-z_{i}^{j}||_{2}+||z_{i}^{j,grid}-\theta_{i}^{*}||_{2}\precsim r_{min}\sqrt{n}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}.

Thus, for
n
large enough, we have

||z_{i}^{j}-\theta_{i}^{*}||_{2}\leq r_{min}\sqrt{n\log(n)}+\xi_{0}\log(n)=r.

Hence, all
\{z_{i}^{j}\}_{j=1}^{t}
lie in
B(\theta_{i}^{*},r)
. Taking a union bound over all
i
, we do not have to truncate at all on an event
\Omega_{5}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{5}|\Omega_{4}^{\prime})\geq 1-\zeta
. Let us work on
\Omega_{5}^{\prime}=\Omega_{5}\cap\Omega_{4}^{\prime}
. Then
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{5}^{\prime})\geq 0.9-\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

Now note that on the no-truncation event,
E_{1}
has independent Gaussian columns, so

\displaystyle||E_{1}||_{2}

\displaystyle\precsim\sigma(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{\log(2/\zeta)})\precsim\sigma(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{\log(2/\zeta)})

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{r\sqrt{n}}{t\sqrt{\rho}}\asymp\frac{r}{\sqrt{\log(n)}}=\frac{1}{n^{2}\sqrt{n}}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)},

with probability at least
1-\zeta
, by Lemma
12
, since
q\asymp k
. Call this event
\Omega_{6}
and let us work on
\Omega_{6}^{\prime}=\Omega_{6}\cap\Omega_{5}^{\prime}
. We also have

\displaystyle||E_{0}||_{2}

\displaystyle=||\bar{Z}-Z||_{2}=\left\|\left[\frac{1}{t}\sum_{j=1}^{t}(\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi)z_{1},\dots,\frac{1}{t}\sum_{j=1}^{t}(\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi)z_{q}\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{q}\mathop{\max}\limits_{i\in[q]}\frac{1}{t}\sum_{j=1}^{t}\left\|(\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi)z_{i}\right\|_{2}\precsim\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}.

Hence, we have
||E||_{2}\precsim\frac{1}{n^{2}\sqrt{n}}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}
, as required.

D.4.7
Proof of Lemma
46

Recall that
Z=[\Pi z_{1},\dots,\Pi z_{q}]
. Thus, the rank of
Z
is at most the rank of
\Pi
, i.e.,
k
. Hence,
\sigma_{k+1}(Z)=0
. Note that the
k^{\text{th}}
singular value can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(Z)=\mathop{\max}\limits_{\mathcal{S}:\dim(\mathcal{S})=k}\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{S},||v||_{2}=1}||Zv||_{2}.

Since
\Pi=UU^{T}
and
U
has
k
orthonormal columns, we obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(Z)=\mathop{\max}\limits_{\mathcal{S}:\dim(\mathcal{S})=k}\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{S},||v||_{2}=1}\left\|[U^{T}z_{1},\dots,U^{T}z_{q}]v\right\|_{2}.

Hence,
\sigma_{k}(Z)
is the
k^{\text{th}}
singular value of a
k\times q
matrix with independent columns distributed as
N(0,I_{k})
, since
U^{T}U=I_{k}
. Hence, by Lemma
12
, we have

\sigma_{k}(Z)\asymp\sqrt{k}\asymp 1
, on an event
\Omega_{7}
with probability at least
1-\zeta
. This is because we assumed
q=C^{\prime}k
(for
C^{\prime}\asymp 1
as large as we like), and since
3k\geq\log(2/\zeta)
, we can upper-bound
\sqrt{\log(2/\zeta)}
by
\sqrt{3k}
, up to absolute constants. Let us work on
\Omega_{7}^{\prime}=\Omega_{7}\cap\Omega_{6}^{\prime}
. Now, by Lemma
5
, we have

\sigma_{k}(Z^{\prime})\leq\sigma_{k}(Z)+||E_{Z}||_{2}\leq\sigma_{k}(Z)+||E||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{k}\asymp 1,

since
\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}=o(1)
. By Lemma
5
, we have

\sigma_{k}(Z^{\prime})\geq\sigma_{k}(Z)-||E_{Z}||_{2}\geq\sigma_{k}(Z)-||E||_{2}\succsim\sqrt{k}\asymp 1.

Hence,
\sigma_{k}(Z^{\prime})\asymp\sqrt{k}\asymp 1
. By the construction of
Z^{\prime}
, its column span is in
\text{col}(Z)
, which is in
\text{col}(P)
, since
\Pi
is the projection onto the columns of
P
. But
\text{col}(P)
has dimension
k
, and we also showed that
\sigma_{k}(Z^{\prime})>0
. Hence, the dimension of
\text{col}(P)
is the same as the dimension of
\text{col}(Z^{\prime})
, i.e.,
k
. Hence, the columns of
Z^{\prime}
and the columns/rows of
P
span the same subspace. We conclude that
\Pi
is also the projection onto
\text{col}(Z^{\prime})
, as required.

D.4.8
Proof of Lemma
48

By Lemma 2.1 of Lei & Rinaldo
[
50
]
, we have
U=\Theta X
, where
\Theta\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
is the membership matrix of nodes and
X=\sqrt{\frac{k}{n}}Z
, for
Z\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
an orthonormal matrix. In fact, we have
D_{P}=\frac{n}{k}\cdot Z^{T}BZ
, for an orthonormal matrix
Z
, so the eigenvalues/singular values of
D_{P}
are
\frac{n}{k}
times the eigenvalues of
B
.

We have, since
||U^{T}||_{2}\leq 1
, that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}(P_{m})

\displaystyle=||P_{m}||_{2}=||E_{m}\Theta XD_{P}U^{T}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq||E_{m}\Theta X||_{2}\cdot\sigma_{max}(D_{P})=||E_{m}\Theta||_{2}\cdot\sigma_{max}(D_{P})\sqrt{\frac{k}{n}}.

Thus, it remains to upper-bound the maximum singular value of
E_{m}\Theta
. Also recall that if the singular values of a matrix
V
are all non-zero, they coincide with the square root of the non-zero eigenvalues of both
V^{T}V
and
VV^{T}
. Note that

(E_{m}\Theta)^{T}(E_{m}\Theta)=\mathrm{diag}(m_{1},\dots,m_{k}),

where each
m_{i}
is the number of times rows from community
i
are chosen in the subsample of
m
rows.
Then
m_{i}\sim\mathrm{HG}\left(n,\frac{n}{k},m\right)
, for all
i\in[k]
. Note that
\mathbb{E}[m_{i}]=\frac{m}{k}
. Let
\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}=\left\{\max\{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\}<\frac{2m}{k}\right\}
and
\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\left\{\min\{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\}>\frac{m}{2k}\right\}
. Therefore, by Lemma
15
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime})^{c}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}\left(m_{i}-\mathbb{E}[m_{i}]\geq\frac{m}{k}\right)\leq ke^{-\frac{2m}{k^{2}}}\leq ke^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime})^{c}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}\left(m_{i}-\mathbb{E}[m_{i}]\leq-\frac{m}{2k}\right)\leq ke^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}.

Hence, on
\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}\cap\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
, we have

\max\{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\}\precsim\frac{m}{k}.

Plugging in gives

\sigma_{\max}(P_{m})\precsim\sigma_{\max}(D_{P})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}},

as wanted.

For the lower bound, recall that the
k^{\text{th}}
singular value can be obtained as follows:

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(P_{m})=\mathop{\max}\limits_{\mathcal{S}:\dim(\mathcal{S})=k}\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{S},||v||_{2}=1}||E_{m}\Theta XD_{P}U^{T}v||_{2}.

For
\mathcal{S}=\text{col}(U)
, which has dimension
k
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{k}(P_{m})\geq\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{S},||v||_{2}=1}||E_{m}\Theta XD_{P}U^{T}v||_{2}^{2}.

Write
U=[U_{1},\dots,U_{k}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
, and let the minimum be achieved by
v^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{k}a_{l}^{*}U_{l}
, with
a^{*}=[a_{1}^{*},\dots,a_{k}^{*}]^{T}
and
||a^{*}||_{2}=1
. Then

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}^{2}(P_{m})

\displaystyle\geq||E_{m}\Theta XD_{P}U^{T}v^{*}||_{2}^{2}=||E_{m}\Theta XD_{P}a^{*}||_{2}^{2}=(a^{*})^{T}D_{P}X^{T}\mathrm{diag}(m_{1},\dots,m_{k})XD_{P}a^{*}

\displaystyle\geq||XD_{P}a^{*}||_{2}^{2}\min\{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\}=\frac{k}{n}||D_{P}a^{*}||_{2}^{2}\min\{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{k\sigma^{2}_{k}(D_{P})}{n}\min\{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\},

where we used the fact that
X^{T}X=\frac{k}{n}I_{k}
and
||a^{*}||_{2}=1
. On
\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}\cap\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
we also have

\min\{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\}\succsim\frac{m}{k},

from which the desired lower bound follows.

D.5
Proofs for Section
5.5
(weighted case)

D.5.1
Proof of Theorem
7

We start with the privacy guarantee. Fix
G\sim_{node}G^{\prime}
, with
G,G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{n}
. To simplify notation, let
\mathcal{A}^{(G)}:=\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))+1)),\delta/(\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))+1))}
. Define

\displaystyle A_{G}

\displaystyle:=(\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G)),\mathcal{A}^{(G)}(T_{w,D}(G))),

\displaystyle A_{G^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:=(\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G^{\prime})),\mathcal{A}^{(G^{\prime})}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime}))),

\displaystyle A_{m}

\displaystyle:=(\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G)),\mathcal{A}^{(G)}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime}))).

Let
P_{G}
,
P_{G^{\prime}}
, and
P_{m}
be the distributions of
A_{G}
,
A_{G^{\prime}}
, and
A_{m}
, respectively.
Let
E
be an arbitrary measurable set in the image of
(\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(\cdot)),\mathcal{A}^{(\cdot)}(T_{w,D}(\cdot)))
. By the privacy of
\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
we have

\displaystyle P_{G^{\prime}}(E)=\mathbb{P}\left((\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G^{\prime})),\mathcal{A}^{(G^{\prime})}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})))\in E\right)\leq e^{\epsilon_{1}}P_{m}(E).

Let
F=\left\{\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))>LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))\right\}
. Since
\mathcal{B}(G)\in\mathcal{G}_{n}
, we have from Lemma
38
that
\mathbb{P}(F^{c})\leq\delta_{1}
.

We need to bound
d_{node}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime}),T_{w,D}(G))
. When altering the weights from one node in
G^{\prime}
, call it
i
, to reach
G
, there are
3
types of changes that can occur:

1.

change a non-zero weight to a non-zero weight

2.

change a non-zero weight to
0

3.

change a
0
weight (same as no edge) to an edge with some non-zero weight.

Say
G
and
G^{\prime}
differ in node
i
. The number of nodes that differ between
T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})
and
T_{w,D}(G)
because of changes
2
and
3
is at most
LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))
. Next, because of change
1
, there can be one additional change. This happens if changes incident from node
i
are not counted by changes
2
and
3
, after applying
\mathcal{B}(\cdot)
, and before adding the weights back. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle d_{node}(T_{w,D}(G),T_{w,D}(G^{\prime}))\leq LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))+1.

(9)

Let
y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
be the pdf of
Y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=5+2d_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))+\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
, so
\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},Y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))\right\}
. If
LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=0
, then
d_{node}(T_{w,D}(G),T_{w,D}(G^{\prime}))\in\{0,1\}
. If
d_{node}(T_{w,D}(G),T_{w,D}(G^{\prime}))=1
, then

\displaystyle P_{m}(E)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}((\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G)),\mathcal{A}^{(G)}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})))\in E|Y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=x)y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}((\max\{1/2,x\},\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(\max\{1/2,x\}+1)),\delta/(\max\{1/2,x\}+1))}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})))\in E|Y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=x)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}((\max\{1/2,x\},\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(\max\{1/2,x\}+1)),\delta/(\max\{1/2,x\}+1))}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})))\in E)y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)dx.

Since
2\epsilon<\log(1/(\delta\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)))
, we have
\frac{2\epsilon}{\max\{1/2,x\}+1}<\log\left(\frac{\max\{1/2,x\}+1}{\delta}\right)
. Therefore, by Lemma
43
, we know that
\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(\max\{1/2,x\}+1)),\delta/(\max\{1/2,x\}+1))}
is
\left(\frac{2\epsilon}{\max\{1/2,x\}+1},\frac{\delta}{\max\{1/2,x\}+1}\right)_{2D}
-node DP. Thus, we have

\displaystyle P_{m}(E)

\displaystyle\leq e^{2\epsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}((\max\{1/2,x\},\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(\max\{1/2,x\}+1)),\delta/(\max\{1/2,x\}+1))}(T_{w,D}(G)))\in E)y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)dx+\delta

\displaystyle=e^{2\epsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}((\max\{1/2,x\},\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(\max\{1/2,x\}+1)),\delta/(\max\{1/2,x\}+1))}(T_{w,D}(G)))\in E|Y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=x)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)dx+\delta

\displaystyle=e^{2\epsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}((\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G)),\mathcal{A}^{(G)}(T_{w,D}(G)))\in E|Y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=x)y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)dx+\delta=e^{2\epsilon}P_{G}(E)+\delta

\displaystyle\leq e^{2\epsilon}P_{G}(E)+\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

implying that

\displaystyle P_{G^{\prime}}(E)\leq e^{\epsilon_{1}}P_{m}(E)\leq e^{\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon}P_{G}(E)+\frac{e^{\epsilon_{1}}}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\leq e^{\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon}P_{G}(E)+e^{\epsilon_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}+\delta_{1}\right).

(10)

Next, if
d_{node}(T_{w,D}(G),T_{w,D}(G^{\prime}))=0
, then
T_{w,D}(G)=T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})
and

P_{m}(E|F)=P_{G}(E|F)\leq e^{2\epsilon}P_{G}(E|F)+\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}.

Now assume
LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))\geq 1
. Then we have

\displaystyle P_{m}(E|F)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{(\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G)),\mathcal{A}^{(G)}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})))\in E\right\}\bigcap F\right)}{\mathbb{P}(F)}

\displaystyle=\int_{LS_{{T_{D}}}(\mathcal{B}(G))}^{\infty}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left((\max\{1/2,x\},\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(\max\{1/2,x\}+1)),\delta/(\max\{1/2,x\}+1))}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})))\in E|Y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=x\right)}{\mathbb{P}(F)}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{LS_{{T_{D}}}(\mathcal{B}(G))}^{\infty}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left((x,\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(x+1)),\delta/(x+1))}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})))\in E\right)y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)}{\mathbb{P}(F)}dx,

where we dropped the conditioning because
Y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))
is independent of
\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(x+1)),\delta/(x+1))}(T_{w,D}(G^{\prime}))
. Also, since
\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},x\right\}\geq LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))\geq 1
, we must have
x\geq LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))\geq 1
. Now fix
x\geq LS_{{T_{D}}}(\mathcal{B}(G))
. Since
2\epsilon<\log(1/(\delta\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)))
, we have
\frac{2\epsilon}{x+1}<\log\left(\frac{x+1}{\delta}\right)
. So, by Lemma
43
, we know that
\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(x+1)),\delta/(x+1))}
is
\left(\frac{2\epsilon}{x+1},\frac{\delta}{x+1}\right)_{2D}
-node DP, and it is clearly
\left(\frac{2\epsilon}{LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))+1},\frac{\delta}{LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))+1}\right)_{2D}
-node DP. By group privacy of size
LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))+1
, we obtain
\left(2\epsilon,\delta e^{2\epsilon}\right)_{2D}
-node DP on groups of size
LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))+1
. Since
\delta<e^{-2\epsilon}/\mathrm{poly}(n)
, this is
(2\epsilon,1/\mathrm{poly}(n))_{2D}
-node DP. Since, when changing
G^{\prime}
to
G
, we change at most
LS_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))+1
nodes to go from
T_{w,D}(G^{\prime})
to
T_{w,D}(G)
, we have

\displaystyle P_{m}(E|F)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{LS_{{T_{D}}}(\mathcal{B}(G))}^{\infty}\frac{\left(e^{2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}\left((x,\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(x+1)),\delta/(x+1))}(T_{w,D}(G)))\in E\right)+1/\mathrm{poly}(n)\right)y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)}{\mathbb{P}(F)}dx

\displaystyle=e^{2\epsilon}\int_{LS_{{T_{D}}}(\mathcal{B}(G))}^{\infty}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left((x,\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(x+1)),\delta/(x+1))}(T_{w,D}(G)))\in E\right)y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)}{\mathbb{P}(F)}dx+\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle=e^{2\epsilon}\int_{LS_{{T_{D}}}(\mathcal{B}(G))}^{\infty}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left((\max\{1/2,x\},\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(\max\{1/2,x\}+1)),\delta/(\max\{1/2,x\}+1))}(T_{w,D}(G)))\in E|Y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=x\right)}{\mathbb{P}(F)}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot y_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(x)dx+\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{(\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G)),\mathcal{A}^{(G)}(T_{w,D}(G)))\in E\right\}\cap F\right)}{\mathbb{P}(F)}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}=e^{2\epsilon}P_{G}(E|F)+\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}.

Now note that
P_{G}(F)=P_{m}(F)
, since in both cases, we have
\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G))
, not
\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G^{\prime}))
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle P_{m}(E\cap F)\leq e^{2\epsilon}P_{G}(E\cap F)+\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle P_{G^{\prime}}(E)

\displaystyle\leq e^{\epsilon_{1}}(P_{m}(E\cap F)+\delta_{1})\leq e^{\epsilon_{1}}(e^{2\epsilon}P_{G}(E\cap F)+1/\mathrm{poly}(n))+e^{\epsilon_{1}}\delta_{1}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon}P_{G}(E)+e^{\epsilon_{1}}(1/\mathrm{poly}(n)+\delta_{1}).

Using inequality (
10
), we deduce that
(\hat{L}_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}(\mathcal{B}(G)),\mathcal{A}^{(G)}(T_{w,D}(G)))
is
(\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon,e^{\epsilon_{1}}(1/\mathrm{poly}(n)+\delta_{1}))
-node DP. Since
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
and
\epsilon
grows with
n
, we have
\epsilon_{1}\leq\epsilon
, for
n
large enough. Hence, because
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
as well, we obtain the desired privacy guarantee.

For the utility guarantee, we rely on the following utility result for fixed privacy parameters, proved in Appendix
D.5.2
:

Lemma 50
.

Assume
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\log(1/\delta)}\geq\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}
,
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{13/2}(n)
, and
s^{2}\asymp a_{n}
. Suppose Assumption
4
and equation (
5
) hold. Let
\theta=\{\theta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the true community assignments. Let
\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon,\delta)}(G)
be the output of Algorithm
8
. Then there exists an absolute constant
C_{1}\asymp 1
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon,\delta)}(G),\theta\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon\log^{4}(n)}\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{5/2}(n)},

with probability at least
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

Let
I:=\left[5+\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}},5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right]
,
I_{min}:=5+\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
, and
I_{max}:=5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
. Let
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}:=5+\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}
. For
F_{1}=\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\in I\right\}
, we have
