\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(F_{1}^{c}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)<-\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)>\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)\leq 2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}.

Let
h_{\epsilon,\delta}=\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\epsilon\log^{4}(n)}
and let
C_{1}
be as in Lemma
50
. Let
f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}
be the pdf of
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
. Also, let
\Omega_{0}
be as in the proof of Lemma
28
. We have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. In addition, on
\Omega_{0}
, we do not have to truncate the graph, since the degree of every node in the unweighted
A
is less than
2d
. Thus, the degree of every node in
W\odot A
is also less than
2d
. Hence, on
\Omega_{0}
, because of the definition of
T_{w,D}
, we have
T_{w,D}(G)=G
and
d_{T_{D}}(\mathcal{B}(G))=0
. Hence, since
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\geq 5>\frac{1}{2}
on
F_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(I_{max}+1)},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(I_{max}+1)},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}+1}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(\max\{1/2,X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\}+1)),\delta/(\max\{1/2,X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\}+1))}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(I_{max}+1)},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}+1}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\cap F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}+1)),\delta/(X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}+1))}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(I_{max}+1)},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}+1}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\cap F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}+1)),\delta/(X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}+1))}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(I_{max}+1)},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}+1}}\middle|X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(x+1)),\delta/(x+1))}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(I_{max}+1)},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}+1}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(x+1)),\delta/(x+1))}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(x+1)},\frac{\delta}{x+1}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since the function
h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(x+1)},\frac{\delta}{x+1}}
is increasing in
x>0
. We also dropped the conditioning on
\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right\}
, since the randomness in
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
only depends on the
\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)
noise, and this is independent of
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(x+1)),\delta/(x+1))}(G),\theta\right)
. We want to use Lemma
50
, with
\frac{\epsilon}{5D(x+1)},\frac{\delta}{x+1}
, and
x\in[I_{min},I_{max}]
. For this, we need
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{13/2}(n)
, and

\displaystyle\max\left\{(C^{(1)})^{2}n^{1-2\beta_{0}}\log(n),\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}\right\}<\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{25D^{2}(x+1)^{2}\log((x+1)/\delta)}\leq\frac{(C^{(1)})^{2}n\log(n)}{4}

(Assumption
4
holds with
\frac{\epsilon}{5D(x+1)}
), which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}<\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{25D^{2}(x+1)^{2}\log((x+1)/\delta)}\leq\frac{(C^{(1)})^{2}n\log(n)}{4},

since
\beta_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
, so for
n
large enough, we have
(C^{(1)})^{2}n^{1-2\beta_{0}}\log(n)\leq\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}
. We already have
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{13/2}(n)
, by assumption. Next, since
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
and
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, we have
I_{max},I_{min}\asymp\log(n)
. Hence, since
2\epsilon<\log(1/\delta)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}(x+1)^{2}\log((x+1)/\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}(I_{min}+1)^{2}\log((I_{min}+1)/\delta)}\asymp\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}\log^{2}(n)\log(\log(n)/\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}\log^{2}(n)\log(1/\delta)}\precsim\frac{\epsilon}{D^{2}\log^{2}(n)}<n.

Hence, for
n
large enough, we have
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{25D^{2}(x+1)^{2}\log((x+1)/\delta)}\leq\frac{(C^{(1)})^{2}n\log(n)}{4}
. Since
\log(1/(\delta\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)))<\Theta(\epsilon)
, we then have

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}(x+1)^{2}\log((x+1)/\delta)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}(I_{max}+1)^{2}\log((I_{max}+1)/\delta)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}\log^{2}(n)\log(\log(n)/\delta)}\succsim\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{D^{2}\log^{2}(n)(\log(n)+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\succsim\frac{\epsilon}{D^{2}\log^{2}(n)}>\frac{1}{\log^{2}(n)},

since
\epsilon
grows larger than
\log(n)
. So, for
n
large enough, we have
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{25D^{2}(x+1)^{2}\log((x+1)/\delta)}\geq\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)}
. Hence, we can use Lemma
50
, implying that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{SE}^{(\epsilon/(5D(x+1)),\delta/(x+1))}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(x+1)},\frac{\delta}{x+1}},

with probability at least
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{1}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{5D(I_{max}+1)},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\int_{I}\left(0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq 0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}-\mathbb{P}(F_{1}^{c})-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq 0.9-3\zeta-2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\geq 0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. Hence, since
I_{max}\asymp\log(n)
,
D^{2}<\epsilon
, and
\log(1/(\delta\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)))<\Theta(\epsilon)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{D(I_{max}+1)\sqrt{\log((I_{max}+1)/\delta)}}{\epsilon\log^{4}(n)}\precsim\frac{D\log(n)\sqrt{\log(n)+\epsilon}}{\epsilon\log^{4}(n)}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{D}{\log^{3}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}<\frac{1}{\log^{3}(n)},

with probability at least
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, as required.

D.5.2
Proof of Lemma
50

The proof proceeds similarly to the proof of Lemma
44
, and it relies on Lemma
54
(in the same way Lemma
44
relies on Lemma
46
).
We work under Assumption
4
and equation (
5
). Fix
1\asymp\zeta\in(0,1/3)
, and assume
3k\geq\log(2/\zeta)
. Also, w.h.p, when dividing
P_{w}=[P_{w,1}^{T},\dots,P_{w,t}^{T}]^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
according to how
W\odot A
is divided in step
1
, all of the
t
chunks have rank
k
. Let
\Omega_{1}^{\prime}
denote the event that all chunks have rank
k
. The following result is proved in the same way as Lemma
45
:

Lemma 51
.

Let
\Omega_{1}^{\prime}
be the event that
P_{w,1},\dots,P_{w,t}
all have rank
k
. Then

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1}^{\prime})\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}.

Let us work on
\Omega_{1}^{\prime}
. Since each chunk has rank
k
, the projection onto the row space of
P_{w,j}
is
\Pi_{w}
, for all
j\in[t]
. Let
P_{w}=U_{w}D_{w}U_{w}^{T}
, with
D_{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
and
U_{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
. Then
\Pi_{w}=U_{w}U_{w}^{T}
.

The following result is proved in Appendix
D.5.3
:

Lemma 52
.

Let
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{13/2}(n)
and
s^{2}\asymp a_{n}
. For all
i\in[q]
and
j\in[t]
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi^{(j)}z_{i}\right\|_{2}\precsim\sqrt{\log(n)},

\displaystyle\|(\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi_{w})z_{i}\|_{2}\precsim\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)},

with probability at least
0.9-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, where

\displaystyle\xi_{0}:=\frac{\log^{1/4}(1/\delta)}{\log^{5/2}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}+\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\log^{5}(n)\epsilon}.

We work on
\Omega_{4}^{\prime}=\Omega_{41}\cap\Omega_{42}\cap\Omega_{43}\cap\Omega_{3}^{\prime}
.

The following result is proved analogously to Lemma
47
, using Lemma
52
:

Lemma 53
.

Let
Z_{w}=[\Pi_{w}z_{1},\dots,\Pi_{w}z_{q}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times q}
. Suppose
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{13/2}(n)
and
s^{2}\asymp a_{n}
. We have
\|\hat{Z}-Z_{w}\|_{2}\precsim\frac{1}{n^{2}\sqrt{n}}+\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}
, with probability at least
0.9-2\zeta-\tau_{w}(n)
.

The following result is proved analogously to Lemma
46
, using Lemma
53
:

Lemma 54
.

Let
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{13/2}(n)
and
s^{2}\asymp a_{n}
. Let
E_{w}:=E_{Z_{w}}+E_{Z_{w}^{\perp}}
, i.e., the decomposition of each column of
E_{w}
as a sum of the component in the column span of
Z_{w}
, and the corresponding orthogonal complement. Let
Z_{w}^{\prime}:=Z_{w}+E_{Z_{w}}
. Assume
\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)}=o(1)
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Then
\sigma_{k}(Z_{w}^{\prime})\asymp\sqrt{k}\asymp 1
and
\Pi_{w}
is also the projection onto the columns of
Z_{w}^{\prime}
, with probability at least
0.9-3\zeta-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

D.5.3
Proof of Lemma
52

Fix
j\in[t]
. By Lemma
51
, the projection onto the row space of
P_{w,j}
is
\Pi_{w}
. We have

\displaystyle||\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi_{w}||_{2}

\displaystyle=||U^{(j),(k)}(U^{(j),(k)})^{T}-U_{w}U_{w}^{T}||_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}2||\sin(\Theta)(U^{(j),(k)},U_{w})||_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}2||\sin(\Theta)(U^{(j),(k)},U_{w})||_{F}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\mathop{\inf}_{Q:Q^{T}Q=QQ^{T}=I_{k}}||U^{(j),(k)}Q-U_{w}||_{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}||U^{(j),(k)}\hat{Q}-U_{w}||_{F}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{(\sigma_{1}(P_{w,j})+||\Pi_{j}(W\odot A)_{j}-P_{w,j}||_{2})||\Pi_{j}(W\odot A)_{j}-P_{w,j}||_{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{k}(P_{w,j})}.

Here,
\sigma_{1}(\cdot)
and
\sigma_{k}(\cdot)
represent the largest and
k^{\text{th}}
largest singular values, respectively. Inequality (a) follows from Lemma
8
. Inequality (b) holds because the Frobenius norm is greater than the operator norm. Inequality (c) holds because of Lemma
11
. Inequality (d) and the existence of the
k\times k
orthogonal matrix
\hat{Q}
hold because of Lemma
10
. Now, we have

\displaystyle||\Pi_{j}(W\odot A)_{j}-P_{w,j}||_{2}\leq||\Pi_{j}(W\odot A)_{j}-(W\odot A)_{j}||_{2}+||(W\odot A)_{j}-P_{w,j}||_{2}.

Note that
\Pi_{j}(W\odot A)_{j}
is the best rank-
k
approximation of
(W\odot A)_{j}
in the operator norm by the Eckart-Young Theorem (cf. Lemma
4
). Now split
W=[W_{1}^{T},\dots,W_{t}^{T}]^{T}
accordingly. Since
P_{w,j}
has rank
k
on
\Omega_{1}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle||\Pi_{j}(W\odot A)_{j}-(W\odot A)_{j}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq||(W\odot A)_{j}-P_{w,j}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq||(W\odot A)_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}]\odot A_{j}||_{2}+||\mathbb{E}[W_{j}]\odot A_{j}-P_{w,j}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq||W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}+||\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A-P_{w}||_{2}.

Hence, since
P_{w}=\mathbb{E}[W]\odot P
, we have

\displaystyle||\Pi_{j}(W\odot A)_{j}-P_{w,j}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2||W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}+2||\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A-P_{w}||_{2}

\displaystyle=2||W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}+2||\mathbb{E}[W]\odot(A-P)||_{2}.

Since
B_{w}\succeq 0
or
B_{w}\preceq 0
, we have
\mathbb{E}[W]=J(B_{w}\otimes J_{\frac{n}{k}})J^{T}\succeq 0
or
\mathbb{E}[W]=J(B_{w}\otimes J_{\frac{n}{k}})J^{T}\preceq 0
. Since
\mathbb{E}[W]
is also symmetric, by Lemma
7
, we have

||\mathbb{E}[W]\odot(A-P)||_{2}\leq\mathop{\max}_{l,g}|\mathbb{E}[W_{lg}]|||A-P||_{2}\precsim||A-P||_{2}.

We also assumed that the entries of
B_{w}
are constant, so the entries of
\mathbb{E}[W]
do not change with
n
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle||\Pi_{j}(W\odot A)_{j}-P_{w,j}||_{2}\precsim||W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}+||A-P||_{2}.

(11)

For
1\leq l<v\leq n
, let
Q^{(lv)}\in\{0,1\}^{n\times n}
be the
0
matrix, with the
(l,v)
and
(v,l)
entries given by the
(l,v)
entry in
A
. Then

\displaystyle W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A=(W-\mathbb{E}[W])\odot A=\sum_{1\leq l<v\leq n}(W_{lv}-\mathbb{E}[W_{lv}])Q^{(lv)},

where
W_{lv}
is the
(l,v)
entry in
W
. Also, for
\zeta_{2}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(||W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}\geq\zeta_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{A}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(||W\odot A-\ \mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}\geq\zeta_{2}|A\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{A}\left[\mathbb{P}_{W}\left(||W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}\geq\zeta_{2}\right)\right],

where the last step holds because
W
and
A
are independent. So we could drop the conditioning, and inside
\mathbb{P}_{W}
, the only randomness is in
W
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(||W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}\geq\zeta_{2}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{A}\left[\mathbb{P}_{W}\left(\left\|\sum_{1\leq l<v\leq n}(W_{lv}-\mathbb{E}[W_{lv}])Q^{(lv)}\right\|_{2}\geq\zeta_{2}\right)\right].

For each fixed
(l,v)
, the random variable
W_{lv}-\mathbb{E}[W_{lv}]
is zero-mean
s^{2}
-sub-Gaussian, with a distribution symmetric around
0
. Also, inside
\mathbb{P}_{W}
, we know that
Q^{(lv)}
is deterministic and symmetric. Then by Lemma
17
, the matrix
(W_{lv}-\mathbb{E}[W_{lv}])Q^{(lv)}
is sub-Gaussian with matrix parameter
C_{1}^{2}s^{2}(Q^{(lv)})^{2}
, where
C_{1}\asymp 1
. Since all the
(W_{lv}-\mathbb{E}[W_{lv}])Q^{(lv)}
matrices are independent and symmetric, we have by Lemma
16
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{W}\left(\left\|\sum_{1\leq l<v\leq n}(W_{lv}-\mathbb{E}[W_{lv}])Q^{(lv)}\right\|_{2}\geq\zeta_{2}\right)\leq 2ne^{-\frac{\zeta_{2}^{2}}{2C_{1}^{2}s^{2}\left\|\sum_{1\leq l<v\leq n}^{n}(Q^{(lv)})^{2}\right\|_{2}}}.

Note that
(Q^{(lv)})^{2}
is either the
0
matrix or the diagonal matrix with
1
’s in diagonal positions
l
and
v
, so

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{1\leq l<v\leq n}(Q^{(lv)})^{2}\right\|_{2}\leq n-1

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{W}\left(\left\|\sum_{1\leq l<v\leq n}(W_{lv}-\mathbb{E}[W_{lv}])Q^{(lv)}\right\|_{2}\geq\zeta_{2}\right)\leq 2ne^{-\frac{\zeta_{2}^{2}}{2C_{1}^{2}s^{2}(n-1)}}.

Therefore, by taking
\zeta_{2}^{2}=4C_{1}^{2}s^{2}(n-1)\log(n)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(||W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}\geq 2C_{1}s\sqrt{(n-1)\log(n)}\right)\leq\frac{2}{n}.

Since
s^{2}\asymp a_{n}
, we obtain
||W\odot A-\mathbb{E}[W]\odot A||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{a_{n}n\log(n)}
on an event
\Omega_{21}
which holds with probability at least
1-\frac{2}{n}
. By Lemma
27
, since
a_{n}\succsim\frac{\log(n)}{n}
, there is an event
\Omega_{2}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{2})\geq 1-\frac{1}{n}
, such that
||A-\mathbb{E}[A]||_{2}=||A-P+\text{diag}(P)||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{na_{n}}
on
\Omega_{2}
. Let us work on
\Omega_{2}^{\prime}=\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{2}\cap\Omega_{21}
. Using inequality (
11
), we have

\displaystyle||\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi_{w}||_{2}\precsim\frac{(\sigma_{1}(P_{w,j})+\sqrt{na_{n}\log(n)}+a_{n})(\sqrt{na_{n}\log(n)}+a_{n})}{\sigma^{2}_{k}(P_{w,j})}.

The following result is analogous to Lemma
48
, for the singular values of matrices subsampled from
P_{w}
. Suppose
P_{w,m}
is a matrix obtained by randomly subsampling
m
rows of
P_{w}
without replacement, i.e.,
P_{w,m}=E_{m}P_{w}
(
E_{m}
selects a uniformly random subset of rows, of size
m
).

Lemma 55
.

With probability at least
1-2ke^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(P_{w,m})

\displaystyle\succsim\sigma_{k}(D_{w})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}(P_{w,m})

\displaystyle\precsim\sigma_{\max}(D_{w})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}},

where
P_{w}=U_{w}D_{w}U_{w}^{T}
is the spectral decomposition of
P_{w}
, with
D_{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
.

Proof.

Using the same proof as in Lemma 2.1 from
[
50
]
, we can derive the following result:

Lemma 56
.

Suppose
G\sim WSBM(n,k,B,\theta,\mathscr{W})
. Let
\Theta\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
be the membership matrix of the nodes. Let
U_{w}D_{w}U_{w}^{T}
be the eigen-decomposition of
P_{w}=\Theta(B\odot B_{w})\Theta^{T}
. Then
U_{w}=\Theta X
and
D_{w}=\frac{n}{k}\cdot Z^{T}(B\odot B_{w})Z
, where
X=\sqrt{\frac{k}{n}}Z\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, and
Z
is orthonormal. Moreover,
||X_{i*}-X_{j*}||_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{2k}{n}}
, for all
1\leq i<j\leq k
.

We now follow the same lines of the proof of Lemma
48
, using Lemma
56
.
∎

By Lemma
55
, there is an event
\Omega_{31}
, with probability at least
1-2kte^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}
(a union bound over all
j\in[t]
), such that on
\Omega_{31}
we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(P_{w})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}\precsim\sigma_{k}(P_{w,j})\leq\sigma_{1}(P_{w,j})

\displaystyle\precsim\sigma_{1}(P_{w})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}.

Let us work on
\Omega_{3}^{\prime}=\Omega_{31}\cap\Omega_{2}^{\prime}
. Recall that
P_{w}=J((B\odot B_{w})\otimes J_{\frac{n}{k}})J^{T}
, and
J\in\{0,1\}^{n\times n}
is a permutation matrix. Then
\sigma_{1}(P_{w})=\frac{na_{n}}{k}\sigma_{1}(B_{0}\odot B_{w})
and
\sigma_{k}(P_{w})=\frac{na_{n}}{k}\sigma_{k}(B_{0}\odot B_{w})
. Hence, because
B_{0}\odot B_{w}
does not change with
n
, we have

\displaystyle a_{n}\sqrt{mn}\precsim\sigma_{k}(P_{w,j})\leq\sigma_{1}(P_{w,j})

\displaystyle\precsim a_{n}\sqrt{mn}.

Therefore, since
d>\sqrt{n}\log^{13/2}(n)
, we have

\displaystyle||\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi_{w}||_{2}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{(a_{n}\sqrt{mn}+\sqrt{na_{n}\log(n)}+a_{n})(\sqrt{na_{n}\log(n)}+a_{n})}{a_{n}^{2}mn}

\displaystyle\precsim\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{a_{n}m}}+\frac{\log(n)}{a_{n}m}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{t\log(n)}{d}}+\frac{t\log(n)}{d}

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{1}{\log^{5/2}(n)\rho^{1/4}}+\frac{1}{\log^{5}(n)\sqrt{\rho}}\asymp\frac{\log^{1/4}(1/\delta)}{\log^{5/2}(n)\sqrt{\epsilon}}+\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\log^{5}(n)\epsilon}=\xi_{0},

for all
j\in[t]
. Now we follow the argument in Corollary 4.6 in Singhal & Steinke
[
64
]
.
Let
\hat{\Pi}^{(j)}
be the projection matrix for the union of the subspace spanned by the columns of
U^{(j),(k)}
and the subspace spanned
by the columns of
U_{w}
. By Lemma
13
we have
||\hat{\Pi}^{(j)}z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{\log(qt)}\precsim\sqrt{\log(n)}
, for all
(i,j)
, on an event
\Omega_{41}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{41})\geq 0.99
. By Lemma
13
again, we have
||\Pi z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{\log(qt)}\precsim\sqrt{\log(n)}
, for all
(i,j)
, on an event
\Omega_{41}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{41})\geq 0.99
. We also have, by Lemma
13
, that
||\Pi^{(j)}z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{\log(qt)}\precsim\sqrt{\log(n)}
, for all
(i,j)
, on an event
\Omega_{43}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{43})\geq 0.99
. Let us work on
\Omega_{4}^{\prime}=\Omega_{41}\cap\Omega_{42}\cap\Omega_{43}\cap\Omega_{3}^{\prime}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle||(\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi_{w})z_{i}||_{2}=||(\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi_{w})\hat{\Pi}^{(j)}z_{i}||_{2}\leq||\Pi^{(j)}-\Pi_{w}||_{2}||\hat{\Pi}^{(j)}z_{i}||_{2}\precsim\xi_{0}\sqrt{\log(n)},

with probability at least
0.9-\tau_{w}(n)
, where
\tau_{w}(n)=t(1-p_{1})+\frac{3}{n}+2kte^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}
. Recall that we assumed
m\geq n^{1-\beta_{0}}
and
1\asymp\beta_{0}\in(1/2,1)
, so
2kte^{-\frac{m}{2k^{2}}}\leq 2kn^{\beta_{0}}e^{-\frac{n^{1-\beta_{0}}}{2k^{2}}}
. For
\zeta,k\asymp 1
, as in Lemma
51
, we have
\tau_{w}(n)\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
.

D.6
Proof of Theorem
8

Regarding privacy, we first establish the following result:

Lemma 57
.

Let
2\epsilon<\log(1/\delta)
. Then Algorithm
6
with privacy parameters
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4D},\delta\right)
is
(2\epsilon,\delta)_{2D}
-DP.

Proof.

By Lemma
58
, Algorithm
6
with privacy parameters
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4D},\delta\right)
is
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{64D^{2}\log(1/\delta)}
-edge zCDP. When changing one node in graphs belonging to
\mathcal{G}_{n,2D}
, at most
4D
edges can be flipped. Hence, by group privacy using Lemma
24
, Algorithm
6
with privacy parameters
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4D},\delta\right)
is
\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4\log(1/\delta)}\right)_{2D}
-zCDP. Finally, by Lemma
21
, we obtain
\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4\log(1/\delta)}+\epsilon,\delta\right)_{2D}
-DP. Since,
\delta<e^{-2\epsilon}
, this is also
(9\epsilon/8,\delta)_{2D}
-DP, and the conclusion follows.
∎

By Lemma
57
, we know that if
2\epsilon<\log(1/\delta)
, then
\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{(\epsilon/(4D)),\delta)}
is
(2\epsilon,\delta)_{2D}
-node DP. Since
2\epsilon<\log(1/(\delta\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)))
Theorem
2
implies that
\widehat{\theta}(G)
is
(\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon,1/\mathrm{poly}(n))
-node DP. We also used the fact that
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
and
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. Hence, for
n
large enough, we obtain
(3\epsilon,1/\mathrm{poly}(n))
-node DP.

For the utility guarantee, let

\displaystyle X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}

\displaystyle:=5+\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}},

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle:=\left[5+\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}},5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right],

\displaystyle I_{max}

\displaystyle:=5+\frac{16\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}.

For
F_{1}=\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\in I\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(F_{1}^{c}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)<-\frac{4\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)>\frac{8\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)\leq 2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}.

Let
h_{\epsilon,\delta}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}n}}+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{a_{n}n\epsilon^{2}}
.

We will use the following result, which applies to fixed privacy parameters
(\epsilon,\delta)
:

Lemma 58

(Adapted from Theorem 5.3 in
[
15
]
)
.

Let
G\in\mathcal{G}_{n}
be sampled from an unweighted SBM (Definition
1
) with
k=2
. We assume
(B_{0})_{11}=(B_{0})_{22}>(B_{0})_{12}=(B_{0})_{21}
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{(\epsilon,\delta)}(G)
be Algorithm
6
evaluated on
G
. Then there exist
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}\asymp 1
, such that for
d\geq C_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{(\epsilon,\delta)}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}n}}+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{a_{n}n\epsilon^{2}}\right),

with probability at least
1-e^{-C_{3}n}
. In addition, Algorithm
6
is
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4\log(1/\delta)}
-edge zCDP and runs in polynomial time.

Proof.

We prove privacy first. By
[
15
]
, the function
\arg\min_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}:X\succeq 0,X_{ii}=\frac{1}{n}\;\forall i}\|Y-X\|_{F}^{2}
has
\ell_{2}
-sensitivity given by
\sqrt{\frac{48}{a_{n}n^{2}((B_{0})_{11}-(B_{0})_{12})}}
. Then the privacy follows directly from Lemma
25
.

Regarding utility, following the same steps as in the proof of Theorem 5.3 in
[
15
]
, we obtain the desired guarantee. The polynomial-time guarantee also follows from the proof of Theorem 5.3 in
[
15
]
.
∎

Let
C_{1},C_{2}
, and
C_{3}
be as in Lemma
58
and let
f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}
be the pdf of
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
. Also, let
\Omega_{0}
be as in the proof of Lemma
28
. We have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. In addition, on
\Omega_{0}
, we do not have to truncate the graph, since the degree of every node is less than
2d
, so
T_{D}(G)=G
and
d_{T_{D}}(G)=0
. Hence, since
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\geq 5>\frac{1}{2}
on
F_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4DI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\mathcal{L}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4DI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{\left(\epsilon/(4D\max\{1/2,X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\}),\delta/\max\{1/2,X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\}\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4DI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\cap F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{\left(\epsilon/(4DX_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}),\delta/X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4DI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right\}\cap\Omega_{0}\cap F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{\left(\epsilon/(4DX_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}),\delta/X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4DI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\middle|X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{\left(\epsilon(4Dx),\delta/x\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4DI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{\left(\epsilon/(4Dx),\delta/x\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4Dx},\frac{\delta}{x}}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since the function
h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4Dx},\frac{\delta}{x}}
is increasing in
x>0
. We also dropped the conditioning on
\left\{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}=x\right\}
, since the randomness in
X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}
only depends on the
\mathrm{Lap}\left(\frac{8}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)
noise, and this is independent of
\mathcal{L}\left(\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{(\epsilon/(4Dx),\delta/x)}(G),\theta\right)
. Since
d\succsim\log(n)\geq C_{1}
, for
n
large enough, we have by Lemma
58
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\mathcal{A}_{TC}^{\left(\epsilon/(4Dx),\delta/x\right)}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4Dx},\frac{\delta}{x}},

with probability at least
1-e^{-C_{3}n}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)\leq C_{2}h_{\frac{\epsilon}{4DI_{max}},\frac{\delta}{I_{max}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{I}\left(1-e^{-C_{3}n}\right)f_{X_{\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}}}(x)dx-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}-\mathbb{P}(F_{1}^{c})-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-2\sqrt{\delta_{1}}-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)},

since
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
. Recall that
d>\log^{3}(n)
and
\epsilon>D^{2}\log(n)
. Hence, since
I_{max}\asymp\log(n)
,
\delta>\frac{e^{-\Theta(\epsilon)}}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, and
\epsilon
is growing with
n
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\widehat{\theta}(G),\theta\right)

\displaystyle\precsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}+\frac{D^{2}\log^{2}(n)\log(\log(n)/\delta)}{d\epsilon^{2}}\precsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}+\frac{D^{2}\log^{3}(n)\epsilon}{d\epsilon^{2}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}+\frac{D^{2}\log^{3}(n)}{d\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}+\frac{D^{2}}{\epsilon}<\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}+\frac{1}{\log(n)}\precsim\frac{1}{\log(n)},

with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, as required.

D.7
Proofs for Section
5.7

D.7.1
Proof of Lemma
3

First note that the release of
\hat{\sigma}_{i}
certainly satisfies
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2k},0\right)_{2D}
-node DP, since the truncation step guarantees that
|\hat{v}_{i}^{T}A_{i}\hat{v_{i}}|\leq D^{2}
.

We will use adaptive composition to obtain a bound on the overall privacy after
k
iterations. Note that

A_{i+1}=A-\hat{\sigma}_{1}\hat{v}_{1}\hat{v}_{1}^{T}-\hat{\sigma}_{2}\hat{v}_{2}\hat{v}_{2}^{T}-\cdots-\hat{\sigma}_{i}\hat{v}_{i}\hat{v}_{i}^{T}.

Note that if we fix the values
\{(\hat{\sigma}_{j},\hat{v}_{j})\}_{j=1}^{i}
, the sensitivity of the score function
v^{T}A_{i+1}v
(for a fixed unit vector
v
) is clearly equal to the sensitivity of
v^{T}A^{2}v
, hence is bounded by
4D^{2}
on graphs in
\mathcal{G}_{n,2D}
. Thus, by adaptive composition, the output after
k
iterations indeed satisfies
(\epsilon,0)_{2D}
-node DP.

D.7.2
Proof of Theorem
9

By Lemma
3
, we know that
\mathcal{A}_{EigDef}^{(\epsilon/(2k))}(G)
satisfies
(\epsilon,0)_{2D}
-node DP. Then by Theorem
3
, we know that
\widehat{\theta}(G)
satisfies
(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon,e^{\epsilon_{1}}\delta_{1})
-node DP. Since
\epsilon_{1}\asymp 1
and
\delta_{1}\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, we obtain the desired result.

Appendix E
Proofs for Section
6

E.1
Proof of Proposition
2

We relabel the community assignments from
[k]
to
\{-1,+1\}^{n}
. By a result of
[
15
]
, we know that

\frac{\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\cdot)}{2}
is a semi-metric over
\{-1,1\}^{n}
, so
\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\cdot)
is a semi-metric over
\{-1,1\}^{n}
.

Let
B_{\mathcal{L}}(x,\ell):=\left\{w=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n})^{T}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}\mid\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}=0,\mathcal{L}(x,w)\leq\ell\right\}
. Pick an arbitrary balanced
\theta\in\{-1,1\}^{n}
, and let
M=\{\theta^{1},\dots,\theta^{m}\}
be a maximal
2\xi
-packing of
B_{\mathcal{L}}(\theta,4\xi)
. The arguments in
[
15
]
, after rescaling
\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\cdot)
, since they use
\frac{\mathcal{L}(\cdot,\cdot)}{2}
, show that
2m\geq\left(\frac{1}{4e\xi}\right)^{\xi n}
. For each
i\in[m]
, define
Y_{i}=\left\{w\in\{-1,1\}^{n}\mid\ \mathcal{L}(\theta^{i},w)\leq\xi\right\}
. For each
i\in[m]
, let
P_{i}
be the SBM distribution for graphs with community assignment
\theta^{i}
. By our accuracy assumption and the fact that the
Y_{i}
’s are disjoint, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{m}\mathbb{P}_{G\sim P_{1}}(\mathcal{M}(G)\in Y_{i})\leq\eta.

(12)

Note that each
P_{i}
is a product of
\binom{n}{2}
independent Bernoulli distributions. If
\theta^{i}
and
\theta^{j}
are two community assignments which differ on node set
S
, consider a coupling
\omega
of
P_{i}
and
P_{j}
which couples all edges not incident to
S
identically.
If
(G,H)
are drawn according to the coupling
\omega
, we clearly have
\mathrm{Ham}_{node}(G,H)\leq\mathrm{Ham}(\theta^{i},\theta^{j})
, where
\mathrm{Ham}_{node}(G,H)
denotes measures the minimal number of nodes needed to be rewired to go from
G
to
H
.

We now use the fact that
\mathcal{M}
is
(K\epsilon,K\delta e^{K\epsilon})
-DP on groups of size
K\in\mathbb{N}
. For all
i\in[m]\setminus\{1\}
, we then have

\displaystyle 1-\eta

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{G\sim P_{i}}(\mathcal{M}(G)\in Y_{i})=\mathbb{E}_{(G,H)\sim\omega_{i1}}\left[\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(G)\in Y_{i})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{(G,H)\sim\omega_{i1}}\left[e^{\epsilon\text{Ham}_{node}(G,H)}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(H)\in Y_{i})+\delta\cdot\text{Ham}_{node}(G,H)e^{\epsilon\text{Ham}_{node}(G,H)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{4\epsilon\xi n}\mathbb{E}_{(G,H)\sim\omega_{i1}}\left[\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(H)\in Y_{i})\right]+4\delta\xi ne^{4\epsilon\xi n}

\displaystyle=e^{4\epsilon\xi n}\mathbb{P}_{H\sim P_{1}}(\mathcal{M}(H)\in Y_{i})+4\delta\xi ne^{4\epsilon\xi n},

implying that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{H\sim P_{1}}(\mathcal{M}(H)\in Y_{i})\geq\frac{1-\eta-4\delta\xi ne^{4\epsilon\xi n}}{e^{4\epsilon\xi n}},

for all
i\in[m]\setminus\{1\}
. Combined with inequality (
12
), we then obtain
e^{4\epsilon\xi n}\geq\frac{(1-\eta-4\delta\xi ne^{4\epsilon\xi n})(m-1)}{\eta}
, so

\displaystyle\epsilon\geq\frac{\log\left(\max\left\{1,\frac{(1-\eta-4\delta\xi ne^{4\epsilon\xi n})(m-1)}{\eta}\right\}\right)}{4\xi n}.

Plugging in
2m=\left(\frac{1}{4e\xi}\right)^{\xi n}
, we obtain the desired result.

For the special choice of
(\xi,\eta,\epsilon,\delta)
, we can make
1-\eta-4\delta\xi ne^{4\epsilon\xi n}\geq\frac{1}{4}
.
Furthermore,
m
increases with
n
if
\xi\in\left[\frac{1}{n},\frac{c_{0}}{n}\right]
, and if
\eta<\frac{1}{2}
, we have
1<\frac{(1-\eta-4\delta\xi ne^{4\epsilon\xi n})(m-1)}{\eta}
, so
\epsilon\geq\frac{\log\left(\frac{c^{\prime}(m-1)}{4\eta}\right)}{4\xi n}
. It is easy to see that
m\asymp\mathrm{poly}(n)
in this setting, and the lower bound becomes
\epsilon\succsim\log(n)
.

E.2
Proof of Corollary
2

By Proposition
2
, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon\geq\frac{\log\left(\max\left\{1,\frac{(1-\eta)(m-1)}{\eta}\right\}\right)}{4\xi n}.

Since
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we have
1-\eta\geq\frac{1}{2}
and
\frac{(m-1)}{2\eta}\geq 1
. Hence,
\epsilon\geq\frac{\log\left(\frac{m-1}{2\eta}\right)}{4\xi n}
. For
n
large enough, we have
m-1\geq\frac{m}{2}
, and we finally obtain

\displaystyle\epsilon\geq\frac{\log\left(\frac{1}{4e\xi}\right)}{4}+\frac{\log\left(\frac{1}{8\eta}\right)}{4\xi n}\succsim\log\left(\frac{1}{\xi}\right)+\frac{\log\left(\frac{1}{8\eta}\right)}{\xi n},

as required.

For the special choice of
\xi,\eta\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, we have
\log(1/\xi),\log(1/(8\eta))\asymp\log(n)
. Hence,
\epsilon\succsim\log(n)
. Next, for
\xi\asymp\frac{1}{\log(n)}
, and
\eta\asymp\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}
, we obtain
\epsilon\succsim\log(\log(n))
, as required.

E.3
Proof of Proposition
3

Let
L\in\{0,1\}^{n\times n}
be an arbitrary deterministic permutation matrix. For any
A
and
\hat{\theta}\in[k]^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(J_{n}^{T}\mathcal{M}(J_{n}LAL^{T}J_{n}^{T})=L\hat{\theta})=\mathbb{P}(L^{T}J_{n}^{T}\mathcal{M}(J_{n}LAL^{T}J_{n}^{T})=\hat{\theta}).

Since
J_{n}
is sampled uniformly, we have
J_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}J_{n}L
. Furthermore, the randomness in
J_{n}
is independent from the randomness in
\mathcal{M}
, so the latter quantity is clearly equal to
\mathbb{P}(J_{n}^{T}\mathcal{M}(J_{n}AJ_{n}^{T})=\hat{\theta})
,
and the definition of symmetry is satisfied.

E.4
Proof of Proposition
4

For every
\hat{\theta}\in[k]^{n}
, we consider the corresponding matrix
\hat{\Theta}\in\mathbf{M}_{n\times k}
. We also make the following definition:

Definition 17
.

The matrix
\hat{\Theta}\in\mathbf{M}_{n\times k}
is a stable approximation of
\Theta
if

\arg\min_{J\in E_{k}}||\hat{\Theta}J-\Theta||_{0}

does not change when any two rows of
\hat{\Theta}
are switched.

Lemma 59
.

If each community of
\Theta
has size at least
6
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\hat{\Theta},\Theta)\leq 1/3
, then
\hat{\Theta}
is stable.

Proof.

Let
J
be the best permutation of communities for
\hat{\Theta}
. Then in a community of size
m
, at least
2m/3
nodes are correctly classified, so if we swap two rows of
\hat{\Theta}
, we still have at least
2m/3-1\geq m/2
correctly classified nodes. Hence,
J
is still the best permutation.
∎

Lemma 60
.

Suppose
\mathcal{M}:\{0,1\}^{n\times n}\to\mathbf{M}_{n\times k}
is a symmetric
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-node DP mechanism. For any adjacency matrix
A\in\{0,1\}^{n\times n}
, any
\hat{\Theta}\in\mathbf{M}_{n\times k}
, and any permutation matrix
J
corresponding to a transposition, we have

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)=\hat{\Theta})\leq e^{2\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)=J\hat{\Theta})+2\delta e^{\epsilon}.

Proof.

The node distance between
A
and
JAJ
is at most
2
, so by group privacy, we have

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)=\hat{\Theta})\leq e^{2\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}\mathcal{M}(JAJ)=\Theta)+2\delta e^{\epsilon}=e^{2\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)=J\Theta)+2\delta e^{\epsilon}.

∎

Lemma 61
.

Suppose
\mathcal{M}:\{0,1\}^{n\times n}\to\mathbf{M}_{n\times k}
is a symmetric
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-node DP mechanism. Fix
\ell\in[n]
, and suppose
T\subseteq\mathbf{M}_{n\times k}
consists only of stable approximations of
\Theta
such that
(\hat{\Theta}J_{\hat{\Theta}})_{\ell}=\Theta_{\ell}
, where
J_{\hat{\Theta}}\in\arg\min_{J\in E_{k}}\|\hat{\Theta}J-\Theta\|_{0}
, i.e., the best permutation of communities for
\hat{\Theta}
correctly classifies the first node. For any adjacency matrix
A\in\{0,1\}^{n\times n}
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)\in T)\leq\frac{1}{1+(k-1)e^{-2\varepsilon}}+2(k-1)\delta.

Proof.

WLOG, suppose
\ell=1
and
\Theta
assigns the first node to community 1. For
i\in\{2,\ldots,k\}
, define
f_{i}:T\to\mathbf{M}_{n\times k}
by setting
f_{i}(\hat{\Theta})
equal to an assignment obtained from taking
\hat{\Theta}
and swapping row 1 with a row assigned to community
i
in
\hat{\Theta}J_{\hat{\Theta}}
. Let
F_{i}
be the image of
f_{i}
. Then
f_{i}
is a bijection
T\to F_{i}
and from Lemma
60
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)\in T)\leq e^{2\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(M(A)\in F_{i})+2\delta e^{\epsilon}.

Since any
\hat{\Theta}\in T
is stable, the best possible permutation for
f_{i}(\hat{\Theta})
is still
J
, so it classifies node
1
to community
i
. Hence, the sets
\{T,F_{2},\dots,F_{k}\}
are pairwise disjoint, so

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)\in T)+(k-1)e^{-2\varepsilon}\left(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)\in T)-2\delta e^{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)\in T)+\sum_{i=2}^{k}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)\in F_{i})

\displaystyle\leq 1,

implying that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(A)\in T)\leq\frac{1}{1+(k-1)e^{-2\varepsilon}}+\frac{2(k-1)\delta e^{-\epsilon}}{1+(k-1)e^{-2\epsilon}}\leq\frac{1}{1+(k-1)e^{-2\varepsilon}}+2(k-1)\delta.

∎

We are now ready to establish the statement of the proposition. Pick an arbitrary community and let
m=\frac{n}{k}
be its size. Let
X
denote the number of nodes in that community that are correctly classified by
\mathcal{M}(A)
(with the best permutation). By Lemma
59
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{M}(A),\theta)\leq\xi)\leq\mathbb{P}(X\geq(1-\xi)m,\mathcal{M}(A)\text{ stable}).

Applying Lemma
61
to each node in the community and summing up, we have

\mathbb{E}[X\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{M}(A)\text{ stable}\}]\leq\frac{m}{1+(k-1)e^{-2\varepsilon}}+2(k-1)m\delta,

so from Markov’s inequality, we conclude that

\mathbb{P}(X\geq(1-\xi)m,\mathcal{M}(A)\text{ stable})\leq\frac{1}{(1-\xi)(1+(k-1)e^{-2\varepsilon})}+\frac{2(k-1)\delta}{1-\xi}.

E.5
Proof of Corollary
3

Taking the result of Proposition
4
and averaging also over the randomness of
A
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{M}(A),\theta)\leq\xi)\leq\frac{1}{(1-\xi)(1+(k-1)e^{-2\epsilon})}+\frac{2(k-1)\delta}{1-\xi},

implying that

1-\eta-\xi\leq(1-\eta)(1-\xi)\leq\frac{1}{1+(k-1)e^{-2\epsilon}}+2k\delta.

Rearranging gives the desired lower bound on
\epsilon
, and plugging in
\eta,\xi,\delta=O\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\right)
easily yields the desired approximation. Additionally, for
\eta=O\left(\frac{1}{\log(n)}\right)
, and
\xi,\delta=O\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(n)}\right)
, we have
\epsilon\succsim\log(\log(n))
, as required.
